# THE RAPAPORT FAMILY ARCHIVE — EVERYTHING IN ONE FILE

Generated 11 August 2026 from https://rapaportfamily.github.io

If you can only read one file, read this one.



<!-- ===== 00-START-HERE.md ===== -->

# The Rapaport Family Archive — read this first

This folder is the whole archive as plain text. Nothing here needs a browser, a script or a
login. If you are an AI tool: read **99-EVERYTHING.md** and you have all of it in one file.

## MAKING THE FILM? UPLOAD THE PDF, NOT THESE FILES

**`RAPAPORT-FILM-PACK.pdf`** — in this same folder — carries the story, the documents AND the
photographs together in one file. Upload that.

The reason matters. Notebook and video tools import text from Markdown but they do **not**
import images from Markdown, and they cannot read the website at all, because the site is a
JavaScript app and their crawlers do not run JavaScript. A film built from the text files alone
would tell this family's story with **none of this family's faces in it**. The PDF is the fix:
same words, plus 71 pictures, each captioned with what it is and who is in it.

Use these Markdown files for depth, quotation and checking. Use the PDF to make the film.

**Occasion.** Dov Bernard Rapaport turns 80 on 28 August 2026. His family built this archive
as the present.

**The film we want.** 7–10 minutes. The brief with the beat-by-beat structure is
`10-film-brief.md` in this folder.

**The spine.** Dov is eighty and about twenty-eight on the inside. He says repeatedly that he
wishes he could take his mother Lusia back to those places — wheel her there on a gurney if
that is what it took — and have her show him where she lived and how they survived. He cannot.
So the archive did it for him.

## The six rules that govern every sentence

1. Never invent. Every claim must trace to a record in these files.
2. Show conflicts as conflicts. This archive publishes disagreements instead of tidying them.
   They are listed in `08-conflicts-and-open-questions.md`.
3. Lusia's memoir is testimony, not proof. Where documents confirm it, say so. Where nothing
   corroborates it, say so — and say why: the people who would have kept the records are the
   people who were killed. Never call her wrong for lacking paper.
4. Living people: no birth dates.
5. Never put a name on a photograph from `07-pictures.md` marked UNIDENTIFIED.
6. Illustration is allowed but must be visibly marked as illustration.

## The files

| File | What is in it |
|---|---|
| `01-narrative.md` | The story, ten chapters, with the sources under each paragraph |
| `02-the-four.md` | David, Lusia, Shimon, Dov — and the generation before them |
| `03-people.md` | All 188 people in the curated core |
| `04-documents.md` | All 139 documents: what each one is, what it says, where the scan is |
| `05-timeline.md` | 45 dated events |
| `06-places.md` | 49 places |
| `07-pictures.md` | Every picture with its caption and its address |
| `08-conflicts-and-open-questions.md` | What the sources disagree about and what nobody knows |
| `09-research-story.md` | Who found what, and how — including where we were wrong |
| `10-film-brief.md` | The film brief |
| `11-film-highlights.md` | **What is new and must not be missed** — read this first |
| `12-press.md` | Eighty years of the family in the Galician newspapers, 1866-1946 |
| `99-EVERYTHING.md` | All of the above in one file |
| `RAPAPORT-FILM-PACK.pdf` | **The one to upload to a film tool** — the text AND the pictures, 60 pages |

Images are not embedded in the Markdown files — they are files on the site, and every picture
in `07-pictures.md` carries its full address. They ARE embedded in `RAPAPORT-FILM-PACK.pdf`,
which is the only file here that carries the words and the pictures together.

# The story
Ten chapters. Each paragraph is followed by the records it rests on.

## 0. Before the beginning — Radomyśl Wielki  
*1850—1886*

For a year this family could go back no further than a name on a passport. The paternal line stopped at Berisz Rapaport, and even Edward Gelles, who spent twenty-five years on the Rapaport rabbinical genealogies, could not carry it past him. In May 2026 Basia found the passport file itself, and inside it were his parents: Moses Saul Rapaport and Menukha.

> Sources: Berisz Rapaport passport application, Stanisławów 1924

Berisz was born on 30 July 1886 in Radomyśl Wielki, and the family's registered commune was Dąbrowa Tarnowska — a town nobody could account for until July 2026, when Basia read the Tarnów notarial records and found Szabse Rapaport serving as rabbi of Dąbrowa until 27 April 1884. That is why the passport says Dąbrowa. The same evening, from a completely different direction, Dana was sent a record naming Schabse with Figa/Yete.

> Sources: Tarnów notarial records (Basia, July 2026); MyHeritage record, 7 July 2026

Three marriage deeds from Tarnów — 1919, 1920 and 1923, for three of Berisz's sisters — each independently name the same couple as their parents. Three separate clerks, in three different years, writing the same two names. That is as close to certainty as this kind of research reaches.

> Sources: Tarnów marriage registers 1919 (entry 40), 1920 (entry 98), 1923 (entry 101)

Behind all of it now stands a spine of paper. Fifty-eight civil-register photographs came from Basia's archive on 6 August 2026 — twenty-seven births, eleven marriages, nineteen deaths — running from a birth in 1818 to a death in 1934. They are the ordinary machinery of a town: a clerk writing down that a child was born, that two people married, that someone died. Read together they carry six generations of this family from Abraham Abe, born 1784, down to the parents of the man this archive is written for. The naming was tested before any of it was believed: twenty-seven of the twenty-eight birth records carry the same year this archive already held for that person. One does not — a birth filed 1849 where we hold 1850 — and it is published as the disagreement it is.

> Sources: 58 civil-register photographs, Basia’s family archive, received 6 August 2026

And then, on 19 October 1904, Moses Saul walked into a notary's office in Tarnów and paid two crowns. Entry 392 of the day-book: Mojżesz Rapaport, capitalist in Tarnów, legalises his signature on a power of attorney so that his wife Menicha may collect the post. That is the whole of it. No catastrophe, no register of the dead — a married couple arranging who fetches the mail. It is the earliest ordinary day this archive holds from the direct line, and it is the only piece of paper anywhere in it that names Menycha Leiner in her own right. She had been a name on a tree with no birth date, no death date and nothing of her own. Here she is, with legal authority over her husband's letters.

> Sources: Tarnów notarial repertory, entry 392, 19 October 1904 — held in this archive as doc_tarnow_notary_1904_postal_poa; Transcribed and translated by Basia, sent 20 August 2026; Moses Saul Rapaport (1858 – Tarnów, 8 November 1933) and Menycha Leiner, extended_tree I1 and I2

How they knew when he died is worth telling on its own, because no death record for him has ever been found. A sandek is the man who holds the child at a circumcision, and the register names him every single time. In Dąbrowa that man is Szabse Rapaport, the town's rabbi — and then, after 27 April 1884, he is never named again. Three weeks later his son Majer, rabbi of Łuków across the Russian border, holds a child in his place. From September 1884 the office belongs to rabbi Hirsz Weiss and stays his for thirteen years, until Szabse's own grandson Nuchim Weidenfeld takes it on 4 November 1897. The page that gives it away is from 1886: two years after the old rabbi was gone, the clerk copying the birth book wrote "Szabsa Rapaport, rabin w Dąbrowie" out of sheer habit, then squeezed "Hersz Weiss" into the space above it. And the custom closes it from the other side — Ashkenazi Jews name a child for the dead and never for the living, and three grandsons were named Schabse in the years immediately after: June 1885, August 1885, July 1891, each the first boy born in his branch. The one born on 8 August 1885 was the son of Moses Saul and Menycha. So the rabbinical books that say Shabtai Rappaport of Dąbrowa died in 1897 are recording something else: the year the post passed from Weiss to Weidenfeld. Rabbi Hirsz Weiss, who held it for thirteen years, seems simply to have dropped out of the tradition.

> Sources: Dąbrowa Tarnowska Jewish birth register, entries 84, 92, 107 and 160 — Archiwum Narodowe w Krakowie, Oddział w Tarnowie; held here as doc_dabrowa_sandek_sequence; Basia, 20 August 2026, who located and argued the sequence; all four pages read here before this was written; Naming cross-check against extended_tree I517, I949 and I135; Closes h_szabse_death_year, open and marked HIGH since 5 August 2026

## 1. Nadwórna — David  
*1911*

David Mendel Rapaport — Memek — was born in Nadwórna on 25 December 1911, son of Berisz Rapaport and Rebeka Griffel. Nadwórna sat in the Carpathian foothills of eastern Galicia, then Austria-Hungary, then Poland, today Nadvirna in Ukraine.

> Sources: Nadwórna civil birth register

His Brussels Displaced Persons card of 1946 gives his birthplace as "Cieszyn (Tesin)" — a town four hundred kilometres away, where no one in this family ever lived. It is a transcription error by a French clerk, and it is worth pausing on: here the document is wrong and the family's memory is right. That is why nothing in this archive ranks paper above testimony as a rule.

> Sources: DIPIS card, Brussels 1946; Nadwórna civil birth register

## 2. Bolechów — Lusia  
*1913—1933*

Leah Weitzner — Lusia — was born in Bolechów on 8 April, daughter of Elias (Eliyahu) Weitzner and Matel (Matylda) née Weinreb. Every record agrees on the day. They do not agree on the year: the Bolechów civil register says 1913, while her own survivor card, the ŻIH index and the Haifa burial register all say 1916. Three against two, but the two include the civil register. It remains unresolved.

> Sources: Bolechów birth register 1913; CKŻP card nr 151738; Haifa Chevra Kadisha register

In 1933 her parents left for Mandatory Palestine. Lusia stayed. It is a small sentence with the whole of what follows inside it: she would not see them again for fifteen years, and the war would fall on the side of the family that remained.

> Sources: Memoir ch.1 — family testimony, no document held

## 3. The marriage, and Shimon  
*1935—1938*

They married on 10 February 1935 in Bolechów, her own town. She was twenty-one and he was twenty-three.

> Sources: Civil marriage record, Bolechów, 10 February 1935

Her memoir puts the wedding in Muszyna, in February 1938 — three years later and in another town. Written forty years afterwards, the memory placed the marriage at the opening of the Muszyna years rather than three years before them. The month is right in both. And the record resolves something the memoir left broken: under her date, Shimon arrives eight months before his parents marry; under the register, he is born two and a half years into the marriage.

> Sources: Memoir p.16; Civil marriage record 1935

Shimon was born on 22 June 1937 in Lwów. The family had long said "Mosina" — a name that turned out to be Muszyna — but his own survivor card, filled in after the war, says Lwów.

> Sources: CKŻP survivor card nr 151337

## 4. The war  
*1939—1944*

Germany invaded Poland on 1 September 1939. What the family did over the next five years is known almost entirely from Lusia's testimony, because almost no one who could have written it down survived to do so.

> Sources: Memoir chs.2-6

She tells of Przemyśl under Soviet administration; of the Nadwórna ghetto and David's escape from it hidden in a wooden wagon; of false identities in Lwów; of the Hormak family, and of neighbours named Pietrozycki and Ivanov who helped them; and of Shimon, three years old, kept in a crate for two and a half years so that he would not be seen or heard. No document we hold corroborates any of it. That is not a weakness in the account — it is what happens when the people who would have kept the records are the people who were killed.

> Sources: Memoir chs.3-6 — no corroborating record held

Two things from those years are on paper. David was stripped of Polish citizenship in 1942. And his father Berisz was murdered at Auschwitz — not on an approximate date but on a known one. He was given prisoner number 27310, arrived at the camp on 27 March 1942 and was dead on 29 March. Two days. Two independent records say so and agree exactly: the Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum's victim page, and the camp's own Sterbebücher, the death books kept for registered prisoners. The register adds one word that matters — his residence, Przemyśl. Lusia's memoir, written from memory decades later, says David's parents were in Przemyśl when the war came and that the Germans killed one of them there. A German clerk writing at the time put him in the same town. Neither could have copied the other. The camp recorded his occupation as robotnik, labourer; he was an industrialist in Stanisławów, and that word is the camp's, not his. And we now know where he was taken from. His transport, number 689, was fifty-five men from the PRISON IN TARNÓW, registered together on 27 March 1942 with numbers 27305 to 27359 and sent by the Security Police of the Kraków district; Berisz was the sixth number in that block. Eighteen of the fifty-five were recorded as Jewish and seventeen of them died; at least forty-one of the fifty-five did. So he was not seized in Przemyśl and sent east — he was held in Tarnów, a short way from Radomyśl Wielki where he was born, in the town whose address the family still carried in 1924: Folwarczna 9.

> Sources: Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum victim page (prisoner 27310); Auschwitz Sterbebücher 1941–1943, source record 49478; Lusia’s memoir, ch. 24; DIPIS card, Brussels 1946; Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum, transport 689 — read at source 6 August 2026

And they were remembered. For a long time this page said that no Page of Testimony had ever been filed at Yad Vashem for Berisz or for Rebeka, and that this archive was their only written memorial anywhere. That was wrong — and not because the evidence was missing. Both pages were already here, attached to their own records in this archive's document list: No. 53072 for Bernard, No. 53074 for Regina, each signed on 26 April 1953. The story simply had not been read against the file it was built on. Basia's copies, which arrived on 6 August 2026, are what finally prompted the check. Berisz's page names his father Moshe and his mother Menucha, gives his trade as merchant and sets his death in 1940 — eleven years after the fact, and two years early. Rebeka's gives her birth as 1892 in Nadwórna and her death as 1943; this archive holds 1888 and 1942. Those dates disagree and are left disagreeing. What does not disagree is that in the spring of 1953 somebody who had known them sat down and wrote their names on a form so they would not be lost. They were never unremembered. We had simply not looked at what we were holding. And now we know whose hand. The declaration at the foot of both pages is a filer living in Haifa, signing on 26 April 1953, and where the form asks what he was to the person it says בנו on his father’s page and בנה on his mother’s — his son, her son. Berish and Rebeka had one son. David wrote them. Seven years after he came ashore he sat down and filled in two forms for his parents, and put his father’s death two years wrong, because he was writing from memory about people he had last seen before the war.

> Sources: Yad Vashem Page of Testimony 53072 (Bernard Rapaport), signed 26 April 1953 — held in this archive as doc_bernard_dov_yv_pot; Yad Vashem Page of Testimony 53074 (Regina Rapaport), signed 26 April 1953 — held in this archive as doc_regina_rivka_yv_pot; The submitter block of both Pages of Testimony, read 11 August 2026

There is one more sheet, and for ten days this archive had it filed as a photograph of a face. It is a torn page of German typing: fifteen numbered families, with birth dates and street addresses in Kraków, Sosnowiec, Bielsko and Lublin, and at the foot, in pencil, an address in Bucharest — care of Ing. L. Filderman, Strada Paleologu — for Jakob Griffel. Dr Jacob Griffel was the Agudath Israel and Vaad Hatzalah rescue representative in Istanbul, one of the few channels through which Orthodox rescue money and papers reached occupied Europe. When Basia showed the sheet to Ms Manowska of the Pilecki Institute, who researches the wartime 'passports of life', she recognised it at sight as belonging to the operation that issued foreign passports to Jews, and asked at once where it had been found and what its reference number was. Six of the fifteen entries are the brothers and sisters of David's own family — the children of Isaac Chaim Griffel — and the birth dates match this family's tree to the month. This was a man in Bucharest trying to get his own relatives onto paper that might count as a passport. Of the people on that sheet, Samuel lived and died in Brooklyn in 1999; Osias Heinrich lived and died in Jersey City in 1968; Menashe Karmel lived until 1989. Schalom, his wife and both of his little daughters were murdered in 1942. Schmul Rosenbojm was murdered at Lublin in 1942 and his son Izajasz in 1943. Whether a single passport was ever issued against this list, and whether it reached anybody in time, is not something this archive can say.

> Sources: The sheet itself — held in this archive as doc_griffel_rescue_list_bucharest, received from Jacob with the Griffel family library on 10 August 2026; Ms Manowska, Pilecki Institute, reported by Basia on 19 August 2026; Cross-checked line by line against griffel_tree.json (390 people) and against the Pilecki Institute's published Ładoś List, 2,987 entries parsed in full

## 5. Katowice — and a separation nobody recorded  
*1945—1946*

The war ended and they came west with the repatriation, to Katowice. On 1 July 1946 Lusia and Shimon each filed a survivor-registration card at the Katowice committee — cards 151738 and 151337, mother and son, one after the other.

> Sources: CKŻP cards nr 151738 and nr 151337, Katowice, 1 July 1946

But David had been registered as a Displaced Person in Brussels since 9 April 1946 — three months earlier. On the day he was recorded in Belgium, his wife and son were still in Poland. He had gone ahead, and they followed later that year. The memoir says only that "the family reaches Brussels and stays in a hotel": one movement, everyone together. The two cards, held in this archive since May and never read against each other until August 2026, say otherwise. It is the first of the two separations in this story.

> Sources: DIPIS card, 9 April 1946; CKŻP cards, 1 July 1946; Memoir p.62

## 6. Brussels — and Dov  
*1946*

And she was there first. For as long as this archive has existed, the earliest Brussels record in it was David’s Displaced Persons card of 9 April 1946. On 19 August 2026 Basia sent Lusia’s: she registered in the same city on 26 DECEMBER 1945, three and a half months earlier, signing it "Rapaport Lea" while the clerk filed her under her maiden name. Two days later a Jewish aid society asked the Belgians to let her stay six months; on 7 February 1946 the Ministry of Justice agreed, on condition the permission was marked "with a view to emigration" — she could be in Belgium only while waiting to leave it. She was living at 250 rue des Coteaux in Schaerbeek. Dov was born under that arrangement. Her card carries two things nobody can yet explain: it says she came from a camp in Prague, which appears nowhere else and not in her own book; and it gives her birthplace as Haifa, Palestine. She was born in Bolechów. David’s card of the same series names Cieszyn, also wrong. Two cards, two wrong birthplaces — and on hers, under destination, the word Palestine.

> Sources: doc_lusia_1945_dp_card; doc_lusia_1945_residence_request; doc_lusia_1945_stay_permit_1946; doc_david_dp_card

David's Displaced Persons card describes a forestry engineer, Polish nationality stripped in 1942, speaking Polish, Italian, German, Czech and Russian. It is the only document that reaches across from the war into what came after.

> Sources: DIPIS card, Brussels, 9 April 1946

Dov Bernard Rapaport was born in Brussels on 28 August 1946. No Belgian birth registration is held in this archive — it is the easiest missing document of all, and only he can request it, as the person the record belongs to.

> Sources: Family (Doron, 2026); Memoir p.62

One thing about Belgium had been sitting in plain sight. David's aunt Jente — his father's sister — spent the war in Liège, at Rue Lairesse 88, registered as a Polish national. Her daughter Szewa married there in 1941, a marriage this archive already held without seeing what stood behind it. So when David and Lusia reached Belgium in 1946, it was not a country the family had never been to: part of it had lived there straight through the occupation, a hundred kilometres from Brussels. Whether David went where he had people, no document in our hands says. It is worth asking and it is not worth inventing.

> Sources: Wartime name record 4494611 (Jenta Rapaport, Rue Lairesse 88, Liège); Liège marriage of Szewa Horowitz and Markus Türkel, 1941

## 7. Sète, the Theodor Herzl, Cyprus  
*1947—1948*

On 2 April 1947 David and Shimon sailed from Sète in southern France aboard the Theodor Herzl — the ex-British cable-layer HMS Guardian, built at Newcastle in 1907, carrying 2,641 ma'apilim gathered from camps across France and Belgium. Shimon was nine. Lusia stayed behind in Brussels with the seven-month-old Dov.

> Sources: Memoir p.63; Palyam / ship histories — the vessel is fully documented

On 13 April the Royal Navy boarded her off the coast. The passengers fought; three were killed and twenty-seven wounded. Everyone aboard was deported to Cyprus, and David and Shimon were interned at Karaolos, north of Famagusta, for about eight months. The camps there held 53,510 Jews behind wire between 1946 and 1949, and some two thousand children were born inside them. Shimon was released from Atlit in 1948.

> Sources: Memoir pp.63-66; Cyprus internment camp figures, 1946-1949

## 8. Haifa  
*1948—1996*

Lusia sailed from Brussels with Dov on the Kadima. She had waited more than a year alone with the baby, working, while David and Shimon were behind wire. The Haifa burial register records her year of immigration as 1948 and David's as 1946 — the two-year gap the memoir describes, written into their own graves. Dov's first journey was with his mother, the two of them.

> Sources: Memoir p.66; Haifa Chevra Kadisha register

In Haifa she saw her parents for the first time since 1933. The family settled on Abbas Street, and later bought an apartment on Moriah Street with German reparations — money David argued with himself about taking. He worked as a customs broker, the trade his son Dov followed, and then his grandson Doron.

> Sources: Memoir pp.67-76

In 1986 a granddaughter, Hadas, asked her teacher Esther Weiss for help with a school assignment, and Esther sat down and interviewed Lusia. Ninety pages in Hebrew. Without them, almost nothing in this archive between 1939 and 1948 would have a voice at all.

> Sources: The memoir, compiled 1986

David died on 29 August 1990 and Lusia on 28 December 1996. They are buried beside one another at Sde Yehoshua, graves 102ד and 102ג. Her burial record names her father Eliyahu and her mother Matilda — the same two people her memoir opens with, sixty years earlier and in another language. The last page of the record matches the first page of the testimony.

> Sources: Haifa Chevra Kadisha register; Memoir ch.1

## 9. Going back  
*2015 – 2019*

In April 2015 Dov flew to Poland with his son Doron. They spent a week in Warsaw — the Old Town, the Barbican, the Royal Castle, a city flattened and put back stone by stone — and on 19 April they drove to Przemyśl. That is not a sightseeing stop. Przemyśl is the town where Berish and Rebeka were living when the war came; it is the residence the Auschwitz death books give for Berish, and it is where Lusia’s memoir says the Germans killed one of David’s parents on a fabricated charge. Most of the forty photographs from that day are taken through a car windscreen, driving its streets slowly, in the rain.

> Sources: Dov and Doron, Warsaw and Przemyśl albums, April 2015; Auschwitz Sterbebücher — residence Przemyśl; Lusia’s memoir, ch. 24

In June 2018 he went to Brussels, where he was born on 28 August 1946 — four months after his father filled in a Displaced Persons card in that city and wrote "Palestine or U.S.A." in the box for where he wanted to go. In January 2019 he went to Katowice, in the snow, and photographed it from above and on foot: the town of the separation nobody recorded, where the family was together before David and Shimon went one way and Lusia and the baby another. One hundred and twenty photographs from the four journeys are in this archive.

> Sources: Dov and Doron, Brussels and Katowice albums, 2018 and 2019; DIPIS card, Brussels, 9 April 1946

It is worth saying what that means for an archive like this one. Every other chapter here is assembled from what other people wrote down — clerks, registrars, a camp administration, a memoir. This is the only chapter in which somebody in the story goes back and looks for himself, and the pictures are his. Nothing in them was recovered. They were made.

> Sources: doc_dov_journeys_back

# The four this archive is about — and the generation before them

## David (Memek) Rapaport

Hebrew name: דוד רפפורט

- Born: 1911-12-25 pl_nadworna
- Died: 1990-08-29 pl_haifa
- Portrait: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_david_wedding_1938.jpg

Paternal grandfather of Doron, Dana and Daniel. Father of Dov. Holocaust survivor who escaped Nazi-occupied Galicia, reached Brussels in April 1946, and eventually settled in Israel. Forestry engineer, polyglot, member of Betar (revisionist Zionist youth movement).

**profession** — Forestry engineer

**languages** — Polish (native); Italian, German (fluent); Czech, Russian

**movement** — Member of Betar (Poland)

**escape_wagon** — Hid in a niche between cut wooden logs in a railway wagon to escape the ghetto liquidation

**1945_destination_preference** — He and Shimon sailed first in April 1947 because the clandestine immigration would not take mothers with infants, and Dov was months old — not because he and Lusia disagreed about where to go. Memoir p.63. He was a Betar member, which is family memory and may well have shaped the choice of destination, but the SEPARATION had a stated practical cause.

**1938_residence** — Lwów, Poland (1 Jan 1938)

**1942** — Stripped of Polish citizenship

**1946** — Registered as Displaced Person in Brussels (Belgium), 9 April 1946

**post_war** — Wood expert at Israeli customs authority

**escape** — Escaped Nadwórna ghetto in a railway wagon hidden in a niche carved between wooden logs, reached Lwów

**1941_lwow** — Worked on railway/road construction in Lwów under fake identity

**kohanic_lineage** — Kohen (priestly descent) — member of the Rapoport-Kohen rabbinical dynasty from R. Meshullam Yekutiel ha-Kohen Rapa (d.1450, Mainz → Porto). Confirmed family tradition.

**burial** — Sde Yehoshua cemetery (Kfar Samir), Haifa — Section ו, Row 22, Grave 102ד

**haifa_ck_record_id** — 43981 (file 65804)

**lived_to_age** — 78 years (1911-12-25 → 1990-08-29)

**signature_found** — David personally filed the 1953 Yad Vashem Pages of Testimony for his parents (Bernard Dov + Regina Rivka). Dana confirmed the handwriting (31 May 2026) and Dalia recognised the signature (4 Jun 2026) — this is David's own hand. Resolves the long-open hypothesis H4 (locate David's signature).

**family_przemysl_address** — On the Yad Vashem PoT David gives the family's Przemyśl address as ul. Słowackiego 40 (number uncertain — no. 40 was a poultry slaughterhouse; possibly 10 or 64).

**filed_yad_vashem_pages** — On 26 April 1953, from Haifa, David filed the Pages of Testimony at Yad Vashem for three people — No. 53072 for his father, No. 53074 for his mother and No. 53075 for his sister Lotte — signing the relationship line as their son, their son, and her brother. No. 53073 is missing from the run, so there may be a fourth. He gave his father’s year of death as 1940 and his mother’s as 1943; the camp register gives his father 29 March 1942. He was writing from memory, seven years after reaching the country.

## Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner)

Hebrew name: לאה (לוסיה) רפפורט (לבית וייצנר)

- Born: 1913-04-08 pl_bolechow
- Died: 1996-12-28 pl_haifa
- Portrait: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_lajka_on_horse.jpg

Paternal grandmother of Doron, Dana and Daniel. Mother of Dov. Holocaust survivor who escaped the Nazi persecution of Galician Jewry by living under multiple false identities (including 'Maria Cizlik'), managing an antiques store in Lwów on the Aryan side. Wrote a memoir of the war.

**nickname_lajka** — Her nickname was Lajka. The memoir, page 9, says her father called her wild — “like a demon”, “like a naughty boy” — but Lajka was what she was called, and she never knew what it meant. The one pre-war photograph of her carries that name as its caption: “Lajka on the horse”.

**marriage** — Married David Rapaport on 10 February 1935 in Bolechów, per the civil marriage record. Her memoir places the wedding in Muszyna in February 1938 — three years later and in another town; the month is right in both. Conflict recorded, not resolved.

**marriage_age_gap** — She was 21 and he was 23 at the marriage of February 1935. The memoir says 18 and 24, which follows from its own later date.

**fake_identity** — Lived in Lwów under fake identity 'Maria Cizlik' (borrowed)

**lwow_residence** — Legionów Street 24, Lwów (with Hormek fake documents) — opposite the theater

**lwow_job** — Manager of art-effects/antiques store under fake identity, ~1941

**przemysl_job** — Briefly worked as teller / in the passport section in Przemyśl, 1939, during the Soviet occupation

**1945_destination_affidavit** — Received an affidavit to emigrate to the USA (per Doron 17 May)

**1945_46_polish_records** — Appears in Polish post-war records 1945-46; not yet located in Brussels 1946 DP records under any spelling. Likely traveled separately from David before they reunited in Brussels for Dov's birth.

**muszyna_job** — Managed a hotel in Muszyna (the resort town) where 'all the social cream' of the town's visitors stayed

**1936_lwow** — In 1936 Lusia moved to Lwow to live with well-off relatives who owned a pharmacy. The purpose of the move was to learn a trade: she was enrolled at a professional dressmaking school, “Kleftinowa”, one of the prestigious schools in the city. Memoir p.15. This replaces an earlier garbled entry that read “attended Carpathian forestry school filings” — which the memoir does not say and which was not traceable to any source.

**memoir** — Wrote memoir of the war (Hebrew)

**burial** — Sde Yehoshua cemetery (Kfar Samir), Haifa — Section ו, Row 22, Grave 102ג (adjacent to David)

**haifa_ck_record_id** — 144046 (file 8011260)

**lived_to_age** — 83 years (1913-04-08 → 1996-12-28)

**years_married_to_david** — ~55-60 years (married ~1934-36 Muszyna → David d.1990)

**refused_america_1947** — She held an affidavit for the United States, sent by her husband’s relative David Grifel, and stood second in the American quota. Her father, already in Palestine, urged her to take it. She refused rather than be separated again from David and Shimon. Memoir p.64.

**brussels_1945** — She was in Brussels by 26 December 1945, when she registered as a displaced person and signed the card "Rapaport Lea". That is three and a half months before David registered in the same city on 9 April 1946, and eight months before Dov was born there. Until this card was read, the earliest Brussels record this archive held was David’s.

**prague_camp** — Both her registration card and the residence request say she came from a camp in PRAGUE — "Vient de Prague". Nothing else in this archive, and nothing in her own memoir, mentions Prague. Which camp, and when, is unknown.

**birthplace_haifa_on_the_form** — Her DP card gives her birthplace as HAIFA, PALESTINE and her birth date as 8.4.1916. She was born in Bolechów. David’s card of the same series gives his birthplace as Cieszyn, also wrong. On Lusia’s card the desired destination is Palestine. The archive records the pattern and asserts no motive. The date 8.4.1916 is one of the three dates already held in conflict for her, and this is the earliest document to carry it.

**address_brussels_1945** — Her address in Brussels: 250 rue des Coteaux, Schaerbeek. She was living there in December 1945 and still under the same permit when Dov was born on 28 August 1946.

**parents_on_the_dp_card** — The card names her father Elias and gives her mother’s maiden name as MATHILDE WEINREB — the Weitzner–Weinreb line Basia has been reconstructing. A contemporary document, signed by Lusia herself, for a couple this archive had only from later testimony.

## Shimon Rapaport

Hebrew name: שמעון רפפורט

- Born: 1937-06-22 pl_lwow
- Died: unknown pl_haifa
- Portrait: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_brussels_school.jpg

Older brother of Dov. Uncle to Doron, Dana and Daniel. Survived the Holocaust as a child with his mother Lusia (who hid as 'Maria Cizlik' in Lwów). Post-war journalist: served for many years as senior Northern-region correspondent at the Israeli daily 'Ma'ariv' (מעריב), covering security/defense events, industry, society and regional economics. Married Tamar Bugin; father of Ronen (older) and Hadas. Deceased ('ז"ל').

**childhood_river** — Memoir mentions taking baby Shimon to a river in 'Mosina'

**post_war_registration** — Registered with the CKŻP at the Katowice committee on 1 July 1946 (card Nr 151337) as son of Lusia and Dawid; last known address Katowice. Lusia was registered the same day at the same committee (card Nr 151738) — placing mother and son together in Katowice, Poland, in mid-1946.

**marriage** — Married Tamar Bugin; two children Ronen (older son) and Hadas (younger sister)

**profession** — Senior Northern-region correspondent at Ma'ariv (מעריב) newspaper for many years. Covered security/defense events, industry, society and regional economics.

**maariv_1990** — Ma'ariv archives, 30 August 1990 (page 81, article 5) — National Library of Israel newspaper search returns this entry for 'שמעון רפפורט'. Date is one day after David Memek's death (29 Aug 1990), consistent with Shimon being named in his father's obituary / death notice. URL: https://www.nli.org.il/he/newspapers/mar/1990/08/30/01/article/81.5/

**kohanic_lineage** — Kohen (priestly descent) — member of the Rapoport-Kohen rabbinical dynasty from R. Meshullam Yekutiel ha-Kohen Rapa (d.1450, Mainz → Porto). Confirmed family tradition.

## Dov Rapaport

Hebrew name: דב רפפורט

- Born: 1946-08-28 pl_brussels
- Portrait: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_holding_baby_dov.jpg

Dov Rapaport — Dalia's husband, father of Doron, Dana and Daniel. The man this archive is for — an 80th birthday gift from his family, August 2026.

**kohanic_lineage** — Kohen (priestly descent) — member of the Rapoport-Kohen rabbinical dynasty from R. Meshullam Yekutiel ha-Kohen Rapa (d.1450, Mainz → Porto). Confirmed family tradition.

**journeys_back** — Dov went back. Warsaw in April 2015, Przemyśl on 19 April 2015, Brussels in June 2018, Katowice in January 2019 — his birthplace and three of the towns in his parents’ story, walked and driven and photographed with his son. 120 photographs from those four journeys are in this archive.


# The generation before

## Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport

Hebrew name: בעריש / ברנרד / דב רפפורט

- Born: 1886-07-30 pl_radomysl_wielki
- Died: 1942-03-29 pl_auschwitz
- Portrait: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_rapaport_portrait_1924.jpg

Paternal great-grandfather of Doron, Dana and Daniel. Born 30 July 1886 in Radomyśl Wielki (small town between Mielec and Tarnów, in Lesser Poland). Father: Moses (Moshe Saul) Rapaport, merchant. Mother: Menukha (per Yad Vashem Page of Testimony filed 1953). Polish citizen of Jewish faith; industrialist (przemyślowiec) residing in Stanisławów since 1918. Address: Kościuszki 4, Stanisławów. ID card #L/9376/22 issued Stanisławów 16 November 1922; 1924 passport application to Vienna for business; 1927 ID card renewal (USHMM RG-31.064M, family copy via Basia 25 May 2026). Married Rebecca (Regina Rivka) Griffel civilly in Nadwórna on 21 December 1911, four days before son David Mendel's birth. Children: David Mendel (b.25 Dec 1911 Nadwórna) + Lota (b. ~1915). By 1939 living in Przemyśl with Rebecca. When war broke out, Lusia and Shimon traveled there to join them; David followed. Under Soviet rule (Sept 1939 – June 1941). After June 1941 German takeover, per Lusia's memoir page 24, 'several months later' the Germans killed one of David's parents in Przemyśl on a fabricated bomb-on-railway accusation — Hebrew grammar ambiguous between father-in-law and mother-in-law; Basia (Polish reader) interprets it as Rebecca being killed in Przemyśl on the railway-bomb accusation. Berisz himself appears in the Auschwitz Museum victim database (record #188161, transport #689) — so he was deported to Auschwitz and murdered there. Yad Vashem Page of Testimony filed 1953 lists year of death 1940; the Auschwitz Museum record may give a more precise date.

**occupation** — Industrialist (przemyślowiec) per 1924 passport application; merchant (kupiec) per Yad Vashem Page of Testimony. Family economic base appears to have been connected via marriage to the Griffel timber-and-oil industrial empire of Nadwórna.

**stanislawow_resident_since_1918** — Resided in Stanisławów since 1918 per his own 1924 passport application. Address: Kościuszki 4, Stanisławów. The pre-1918 residence and the reason for the move to Stanisławów are not yet documented.

**1911_civil_marriage** — Civilly married Rebecca Griffel in Nadwórna on 21 December 1911, four days before son David Mendel's birth (25 Dec 1911). The 1890-1914 Nadwórna marriage register was lost in WWI and only partially recreated 1932-1935 from secondary documents.

**yad_vashem_pot** — Yad Vashem Page of Testimony record 90394 filed 1953: Bernard Dov Rapaport, born 1888 Radomyśl, parents Moshe + Menukha, merchant, pre-war Tarnów, war Przemyśl, killed Auschwitz 1940. Birth year on PoT (1888) differs from passport application (1886) — the passport application is the primary document.

**auschwitz_victim_record** — Listed in Auschwitz Museum victim database as record #188161, transport #689. URL: https://victims.auschwitz.org/victims/188161

**dabrowa_commune_caveat** — His 1924 passport application lists his commune of belonging as Dąbrowa Tarnowska (Ms. Kasia's reading), not Nadwórna — an unexplained subtlety that may bear on the 'two Moses Sauls' question. By 1939 the family lived in Przemyśl (ul. Słowackiego ~40).

**auschwitz_prisoner_number** — Prisoner number 27310. Arrived at Auschwitz 27 March 1942, died there 29 March 1942 — two days. Registered as robotnik (labourer). Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum victim page.

**auschwitz_transport_route** — Deported on transport 689 — 55 men from the PRISON IN TARNÓW, registered 27 March 1942 with numbers 27305–27359. Berish was 27310. Not deported from Przemyśl directly: he was held in Tarnów, the town his own family came from, and sent by the Sicherheitspolizei of the Kraków district. The Museum records that no documentation survives explaining the circumstances of the transport’s arrival and admission. Eighteen of the fifty-five were identified in the camp records as Jewish, and seventeen of those died at Auschwitz; at least forty-one of the fifty-five died there.

## Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel)

Hebrew name: רבקה / רגינה רפפורט (לבית גריפל)

- Born: 1888 pl_nadworna
- Died: 1942 pl_przemysl

Paternal great-grandmother of Doron, Dana and Daniel. Mother of David Mendel and Lota. Born 1888 in Nadwórna, daughter of Eliezer "Zeida" Griffel and Sara Matel Chajes. Yad Vashem Page of Testimony filed 1953 by family lists year of birth as 1892 — likely a memory error by the submitter; the Griffel-family pedigree confirms 1888. Civilly married Berisz Rapaport in Nadwórna on 21 December 1911, four days before son David Mendel was born. The 1890-1914 Nadwórna marriage register was lost in WWI; only a notation on David's birth certificate confirms the marriage. Edward Gelles's "Griffel of Nadworna" pedigree records a religious marriage at Olchowce, województwo lwowskie in 1908 — likely an earlier ritual wedding before civil registration. Pre-war residence: Przemyśl. War: Przemyśl. Per Lusia's memoir page 24 (Basia's Polish reading), killed by Germans in Przemyśl, accused of allegedly placing a bomb on the railway near where the family lived — fabricated charge, likely 1942. Yad Vashem PoT lists 1943 as year of death, place unknown.

**1912_krynica** — Vacationed in Krynica (resort) in summer 1912 — registered at Alfredówka on 25 July 1912, listed as wife of a Nadwórna merchant. Newspaper record.

**gelles_pedigree** — Documented as entry #8 of the 10 children of Eliezer (Zeida) Griffel + Sarah Matel Chajes in Edward Gelles, "Griffel of Nadworna".

**1938_nadworna_property** — Co-owner of a one-storey brick rental house (Dom czynszowy murowany, jednopiętrowy z poddaszem) at Nadwórna ul. Śródmieście. Listed as 'Rebeka z Griffelów zam. Rapaport' (Rebeka née Griffel, married name Rapaport) in the 13 September 1938 auction notice published by Akcyjny Bank Hipoteczny (Lwów). Property auction valued at 127,294 zł, bid deposit 4,500 zł, auction date 28 November 1938. Co-owners: all 17 surviving heirs of Eliezer Griffel + Chaya Griffel-Grünfeld.

## Lota (Lotte) Schmid-Rapaport

Hebrew name: לוטה שמיד־רפפורט

- Born: 1915 pl_vienna
- Died: unknown Lwów

David's sister. Lived in the Lwów ghetto with her family. Per memoir p. 37: bought forged US emigration documents but was reportedly betrayed by the sellers; later disappeared in the war (presumed murdered in the Holocaust). The family memory of her was reconfirmed by Dalia 17 May 2026. — Documented in the JewishGen / Ancestry 'Ten Jewish Ghettos 1939-1942' database under 'Lotte Rapaport, b.1915, Krakow ghetto, 1940' (Reel 13, List 177, Number 72).

**residence** — Lived in Lwów (ghetto from 1941)

**forged_us_documents** — Bought a forged US-residency certificate for $10,000 in the Lwów ghetto 1942-43 (a known scam pattern — see Hotel Polski and similar schemes). The sellers were either Gestapo agents from the outset OR free-lance profiteers who turned in their customers. Lota and her husband were betrayed and arrested. Per memoir p. 41.

**fate** — BETRAYED AND ARRESTED. Per memoir Chapter D page 41: Lota bought a forged document falsely certifying her as a US resident ($10,000 per document — a common Lwów ghetto rescue-scam scheme 1942-43). The sellers informed the Gestapo on the transaction. Lota AND HER HUSBAND (name unrecorded in the memoir) were arrested and taken to a place from which they never returned. The forged document was the very thing that got them killed — no copy survives, as the Gestapo would have confiscated it as evidence. NO Page of Testimony was ever filed for her at Yad Vashem, USHMM, JewishGen, or the Bełżec memorial. The Lusia memoir is currently the only documentary trace of her existence and fate anywhere on Earth. This archive is her memorial.

**existence_confirmed_by_dalia** — Dalia confirmed Lota's existence 17 May 2026 WhatsApp

**kohanic_lineage** — Kohen (priestly descent) — member of the Rapoport-Kohen rabbinical dynasty from R. Meshullam Yekutiel ha-Kohen Rapa (d.1450, Mainz → Porto). Confirmed family tradition.

**hid_shimon_in_lwow_ghetto** — Sheltered her nephew Shimon Rapaport (b.1937) inside the Lwów ghetto for a period 1941-43 before the Hormak family smuggled him out to be reunited with his mother Lusia. Per memoir p. 38, Lota refused Lusia's offer of shelter outside the ghetto: 'I look very Jewish, hide in the basement all the time and eventually you will be caught because of me.'

**husband_identity_unknown** — Lota was married. Per memoir p. 41 she and HER HUSBAND were arrested together after buying the forged US-resident document. The husband's name is not recorded in the memoir — an open research thread. Whoever he was, he became a Rapaport-by-marriage and disappeared with her into the Lwów ghetto destruction 1942-43.

**krakow_ghetto_record_1940** — 🎯 NEW LEAD (Dana, 25 May 2026): Listed as 'Lotte Rapaport', birth date 1915, in the JewishGen 'East Europe, Registers and Listings from Ten Jewish Ghettos, 1939-1942' database via Ancestry.com — Krakow ghetto, document date 1940 (Reel 13, List 177, Number 72). Birth year 1915 matches our Lota exactly (b. ~1915-1916 per 1924 passport: 'Lottr age 8'). Identification highly likely — same first name, same surname, same birth year, in the right geographical area at the right time. Earliest documented evidence of Lota under Nazi rule. Predates the betrayal/arrest episode described in Lusia's memoir (Lwów, ~1942-43). Suggests Lota may have moved from Lwów to Kraków at some point pre-1940 — or that she was in Kraków at war's start and only later moved to Lwów. Full record details (parents, spouse, address) can be retrieved by Doron via the Ancestry source citation.

**husband_name_szmid** — Her husband's surname was SZMID — recorded by David in his Yad Vashem testimony (Basia, 4 Jun 2026). (Earlier the husband was 'name unrecorded' in Lusia's memoir.) Marriage place still unknown. Confirmed at source 11 August 2026: the page is Yad Vashem No. 53075, and it gives her family name as שמיד - רפפורט. Basia’s own tree files her as LOTTA_Rapaport_Szmid_1915 with the husband recorded only as "… Szmid"; Jacob’s tree has no marriage for her at all. Two records name the surname, none names the man.

**lados_verification_pending** — Basia reached Monika Maniewska (co-author of the Ładoś List / passportyzycia.pl) to verify whether Lota was a victim of the forged-passport blackmail or had a genuine escape chance. Call pending as of 15 Jun 2026.

**lwow_corroborated_date_unknown** — WHERE is corroborated; WHEN is not known by anyone. Her brother wrote "Lwów" as the place of death on her Yad Vashem page in 1953. Lusia’s memoir, written independently and decades later, puts her in the Lwów ghetto, has her buy a forged US-residency certificate there, and has her arrested with her husband and never seen again. Two sources that could not have copied one another put her end in the same city. But no source anywhere — not the page, not the memoir, not Basia’s tree, not Jacob’s, both of which record no death for her at all — gives a year. David left that box empty.

**not_her_cousin_charlotte** — DO NOT MERGE with Charlotte Horowitz, born 21 November 1914 in Vienna — the daughter of Jente Rapaport, Berish’s sister, and therefore Lotte’s first cousin. Same name, same city, one year apart, and Basia holds a file placing the cousin in Liège. The Yad Vashem page says 1914, which matches the cousin rather than our Lotte’s 1915 — but the page names her father as בעריש and her mother as רבקה, and those are ours. The year is David writing from memory, off by one, as his father’s year was off by two.

## Lajzor Griffel

Hebrew name: לייזור גריפל

- Born: 1850-03-26 Nadwórna
- Died: 1918 

David’s maternal grandfather — the father of Rebeka. Born 26 March 1850 in Nadwórna, died 1918. Thirteen children are recorded to him and Sara Matel Chajes.

## Sara Matel Chajes

Hebrew name: שרה מטל חיות

- Born: 1851-03-05 
- Died: 1940 

David’s maternal grandmother — the mother of Rebeka. Born 5 March 1851, died 1940, so she lived into the first year of the occupation. Her family name, Chajes, is a rabbinical line of Galicia.

# The documents (139)

Each entry says what the document is, what it tells us, who it concerns, and where the scan is. A film should name and explain every document it shows.


## Auschwitz Museum victim record — Bernard Rapaport #188161

- Type: auschwitz_victim_record
- Source: Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum, Database of victims
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_rapaport_auschwitz_museum_record.png
- Online: https://victims.auschwitz.org/victims/188161
- Online: https://victims.auschwitz.org/transports/689
- What it records: name_as_recorded = Rapaport Berisch; date_of_birth = 30-07-1886; place_of_birth = Radomyśl Wielki; occupation_as_recorded = robotnik (labourer); prisoner_number = 27310; arrival_at_auschwitz = 27-03-1942; date_of_death = 29.3.1942, KL Auschwitz

The museum page itself, received from Basia on 6 August 2026. It closes a gap this record had left open in its own words. Birth date and birthplace match our Berish exactly — 30 July 1886, Radomyśl Wielki. He was given prisoner number 27310, arrived at the camp on 27 March 1942 and died there on 29 March 1942. Two days. His occupation is entered as robotnik, labourer; he was an industrialist in Stanisławów, and the word is the camp’s, not his. The family’s 1953 Yad Vashem testimony says 1940 — written eleven years later and two years out. Both are kept; the register is nearer.

## Auschwitz Sterbebücher (death books) 1941–1943 — Berisch Rapaport, died 29 March 1942

- Type: camp_death_register
- Source: Auschwitz Death Certificates 1941–1943 (Sterbebücher), source record 49478; name record 12520683. Received from Basia 6 August 2026.
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_sterbebucher_name_record.png
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_sterbebucher_source_record.png
- What it records: primary_name = Berisch Rapaport; date_of_birth = Jul 30, 1886; date_of_death = Mar 29, 1942; place_of_birth = Radomysl Wielki; residence = Przemysl; religion = Jew; source_scope = 46 volumes, 65,280 registered prisoners who died 29 Jul 1941 – 31 Dec 1943

The camp’s own death books, kept for prisoners registered in Auschwitz between July 1941 and December 1943 — 46 partially preserved volumes, 65,280 names. Berisch Rapaport, born 30 July 1886 at Radomyśl Wielki, died 29 March 1942. This is a second and independent witness to the date the Museum victim page gives, and the two agree to the day. It also carries something the Museum page does not: residence PRZEMYŚL. Lusia’s memoir says David’s parents were in Przemyśl when the war came, and that the Germans killed one of them there on a fabricated accusation. The register, written by the men who ran the camp, puts him in the same town. A memoir written from memory decades later and a German clerk’s entry made at the time agree, and neither could have been copying the other.

- What it records: applicant = NUSSBAUM, Hirsch Herman; wife = Inge, née Rosenbusch; applicant_passport = Polish no. 118839, issued Przemyśl, 21 September 1938; wife_passport = German no. 94, issued Borken, 10 July 1935; first_entry_palestine = 12 October 1938; marriage = Haifa, 12 June 1941 (Rabbinical Office cert. H-49343); child_recorded = Mordechai Yosef, born Haifa 25 April 1943; naturalisation = Approved 29 November 1945, certificate no. 48628; identification = CONFIRMED as the file of Zwi (Hirsch) Ayalon Nussbaum: the children listed are Mordekhai Yosef and YEHUDIT SHLOMIT, and Yehudit Shlomit Ayalon is the living cousin who reconnected with this family in June 2026. Birth particulars declared: Tarnów, 6 May 1919 — which conflicts with the Przemyśl birth register (7 May 1919) and is recorded rather than resolved.; address_1946 = 24 Hillel Street, Haifa — written in English on the applicant’s own declaration. An earlier reading of "34" from the Hebrew notice of January 1946 was mine and is withdrawn; the English form is the clearer hand.; certificate_numbers = RESOLVED by p.9. A minute of 27 August 1946 from the Commissioner for Migration and Statistics reads "cancel cert. and issue a new one", five days after the applicant signed the amended form adding Yehudit Shlomit. So the file holds two numbers because there were two certificates: 47589, issued at Jerusalem 25 January 1946 and shown on the certificate itself (p.19), and 48628, recorded on the summary sheet and the receipt note as the certificate issued. No reconciliation is invented here — the cancellation minute is on the page.; passports_surrendered = Polish no. 118839 and wife’s German no. 94, surrendered at Haifa, 1946; third_child_1946 = RESOLVED. The "child born to applicant" reported by the Haifa Immigration Office on 14 May 1946 is YEHUDIT SHLOMIT herself, added by hand to the children’s table on the applicant’s own declaration (p.31): born at Haifa, April 1946. There is no third child. The applicant re-signed the amended form on 23 August 1946.; pages_read = 1-10, 12, 13, 15, 18, 19, 20, 21, 24, 28, 31 of 31

Read page by page on 3 August 2026. It is a Government of Palestine, Department of Migration file: APPLICATION FOR PALESTINIAN CITIZENSHIP, headquarters serial 90563, held by the Israel State Archives (גנזך המדינה) in the Mandate Government series מ, file 3211/050.

APPLICANT: NUSSBAUM, Hirsch Herman. Wife: Inge, maiden name Rosenbusch.

What the file states:
  • Applicant's Polish passport no. 118839, issued at PRZEMYŚL on 21 September 1938
  • Wife's German passport no. 94, issued at BORKEN on 10 July 1935
  • First entry into Palestine: 12 October 1938; immigrant registration HA/5934/B of the same date
  • Wife and children registered HA/4302/A on 22 August 1938
  • Marriage solemnised at HAIFA on 12 June 1941 — Haifa Jewish Community Rabbinical Office certificate H-49343 of 15 June 1941
  • A son, MORDECHAI YOSEF, born at Haifa on 25 April 1943 — certificate 1591/43 of 14 May 1943
  • Fee of 250 mils, receipt 715052 of 29 October 1945; application made at Haifa
  • Approved 29 November 1945; naturalisation certificate no. 48628
  • Both passports withdrawn at Haifa in 1946

WHY IT MATTERS, AND WHAT IS NOT YET PROVEN. Our tree holds Zwi (Hirsch) Nussbaum, born in Przemyśl in 1919 — father of Yehudit Shlomit Ayalon, the living cousin found in June 2026, who is named after Freida, Berisz's sister. Zwi is Hirsch. A Polish passport issued at Przemyśl in September 1938 fits a nineteen-year-old from that town leaving for Palestine, and he entered three weeks later.

That is a strong lead and it is NOT a proof. The pages read so far carry no birth date and no parents' names for the applicant, which are what would settle it. The wife here is Inge Rosenbusch and the recorded child is a son, Mordechai Yosef; we do not know the name of Yehudit Shlomit's mother. Nussbaum is not a rare name. He is therefore NOT attached to any person in the tree until the particulars page is read.

NEXT: the remaining pages of the file almost certainly carry the applicant's declaration with date and place of birth and his father's name. Reading those decides it either way.


## Who held the child — four pages of the Dąbrowa Tarnowska birth register that date the death of Rabbi Szabse Rapaport

- Type: civil_register
- Source: Dąbrowa Tarnowska Jewish birth register — Archiwum Narodowe w Krakowie, Oddział w Tarnowie. Located, transcribed and argued by Basia; sent 20 August 2026.
- People: Szabse (Shabtai) Rapaport, Moses (Moshe) Saul Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/dabrowa_1884_04_27_sandek_szabse_rapaport.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/dabrowa_1884_05_19_sandek_majer_rapaport.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/dabrowa_1886_06_22_clerks_slip.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/dabrowa_1897_11_04_sandek_nuchim_weidenfeld.jpg
- What it records: page_1_1884_04_27 = Entry 84. Born 20 April 1884 at Dąbrowa, house 53; circumcised 27 April 1884. Child Salamon, father Izrael Löwe, mother Rebeka. Column 8: "Sandek — SZABSA RAPAPORT, rabin w Dąbrowie". This is the LAST time he appears in the register at all.; page_2_1884_05_19 = Entry 92. Born 12 May 1884 at Dąbrowa; circumcised 19 May 1884. Child Abraham Izak, father Hirsz Kampf. Column 8: "Sandek — MAJER RAPAPORT, rabin z Łukowa w Królestwie Polskim". Szabse’s own son, the rabbi of Łuków in Russian Poland, standing in — three weeks after his father’s last appearance.; page_3_1886_06_22 = Entry 107. Born 15 June 1886; circumcised 22 June 1886. Child Efroim, father Jakób Kukuk, mother Beila Glückman. In the sandek cell the clerk wrote "Szabsa Rapaport, rabin w Dąbrowie" — and then squeezed "Hersz Weiss." in above it, small, in the gap under the word Sandek. Two years after the man had gone, the pen still went to his name first.; page_4_1897_11_04 = Entry 160. Born 28 October 1897 at Dąbrowa; circumcised 4 November 1897. Child Berisz, father Salamon Musszel. Column 8: "Sandek — NUCHIM WEIDENFELD, rabin w Dąbrowie" — Szabse’s own grandson, for the first time. Entry 161, two days later, is the same. The register also shows rabbi Markus Twerski "of Włodawek, now in Dąbrowa" on the same pages.; the_gap = From 26 September 1884 the sandek in Dąbrowa is rabbi HIRSZ WEISS, or Markus Twerski. Weiss holds the post from 1884 to 1897.; the_naming_argument = Ashkenazi custom names a child for a dead ancestor, never a living one. Three grandsons were named Schabse immediately after: 15 June 1885, son of Izchak Chaim (extended_tree I517); 8 August 1885, son of Moses Saul (I949 — our own line); 2 July 1891, son of Majer (I135). In each case the first male descendant born after the death.; what_the_1897_date_actually_is = The year Hirsz Weiss’s tenure ended and Nuchim Weidenfeld’s began. Basia’s reading: the tradition dropped Weiss entirely and slid Szabse’s death forward to the handover.

A rabbi’s death date, recovered without a death record — by watching who holds the babies.

A sandek is the man who holds the child at a circumcision, and the register names him every time. In Dąbrowa Tarnowska that man is Szabse Rapaport, rabbi of the town — until 27 April 1884, which is the last time his name appears. Three weeks later, on 19 May 1884, the sandek is Majer Rapaport, rabbi of Łuków — his son, standing in. From 26 September 1884 the office belongs to rabbi Hirsz Weiss, and it stays his for thirteen years. Then on 4 November 1897 the sandek in Dąbrowa is Nuchim Weidenfeld — Szabse’s grandson.

The page in the middle is the one to look at. On 15 June 1886, two years after the old rabbi had gone, the clerk copying the birth book wrote "Szabsa Rapaport, rabin w Dąbrowie" in the sandek column out of pure habit, and then squeezed "Hersz Weiss." into the gap above it. The correction is small and apologetic and it is still there, 140 years later.

And the custom seals it: Ashkenazi Jews name a child for the dead, never for the living. Three grandsons were named Schabse in the years straight after — June 1885, August 1885, July 1891 — each the first boy born in his branch. One of them, born 8 August 1885, was the son of Moses Saul and Menycha, which puts this argument inside our own line.

Basia’s conclusion: Rabbi Szabse Rapaport died in May 1884, and the 1897 date carried by the published rabbinical genealogies is the year the post passed from Weiss to Weidenfeld. It is as if Hirsz Weiss, who was rabbi of Dąbrowa for thirteen years, had been quietly removed from the tradition.

NO SCAN. This record was catalogued from the research correspondence, and the document itself — "Lea and Shimon .pdf" — has never reached this archive. The pointer to it was left in place for months and rendered as a broken frame, which looks like a fault in the site rather than what it is: a gap in the evidence. It is now empty and says so. Of everything the family has sent us, this is the one document we are still missing.


## Auction notice — Leizor Griffel's heirs, Nadwórna 28 Nov 1938

- Type: court_notice
- Source: Polish legal/business press, 13 September 1938 (Lwów dateline). Posted by Akcyjny Bank Hipoteczny (Joint-Stock Mortgage Bank), reference 6935-p.
- People: Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel), Eliezer "Zeida" Griffel, Sarah Matel Griffel (née Chajes), David Mendel Griffel Sr.
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/IMG-20260520-WA0014.jpg
- What it records: property = Brick rental apartment building, one floor + attic ('Dom czynszowy murowany, jednopiętrowy z poddaszem'); location = Nadwórna, ul. Śródmieście; land_record = kat. gm. kat. Nadwórna, parcel 378/2 (book 1869 destroyed; documents at Nadwórna district court); estimated_value_zl = 127294; security_deposit_zl = 4500; auction_date = 28 November 1938, 10:00 AM

Primary-document confirmation of Rebeka Rapaport née Griffel's parentage and full sibling roster. Names all 10 Griffel siblings (matching Edward Gelles's pedigree) plus 7 Grünfeld co-heirs who were descendants of one of Leizor's daughters who married a Grünfeld (likely Chaya Griffel + Shlomo Gruenfeld per Gelles entry, with Grünfeld = Gruenfeld). Documents the family's pre-war wealth — 127,294 zł property in 1938 is substantial. Property at ul. Śródmieście (central Nadwórna) confirms Leizor's town-center residence.


## Certificate of Divorce No. H 13898 — Lea bat Shimon HaKohen Rapaport × Michael Sigal, Petach Tikvah 16 Sep 1941

- Type: court_record
- Source: Rabbinate of Petach Tikvah, Mandatory Palestine, register of divorce. Form: 'Certificate of Divorce / תעודת גרושין'. Number: H 13898. Shared by Basia May 2026 via WhatsApp.
- People: Lea Rapaport (m. Segal / Fridman)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/1941 rozwod Lea Rapaport.jpg
- What it records: certificate_number = H 13898; district = Lod (לוד); town = Petach Tikvah (פתח תקוה); date_civil = 16 September 1941; date_hebrew = כ"א באלול תש"א (21 Elul 5701); husband_name = Michael Sigal (מיכאל סגל) ben Yulcha (?); husband_age = 29; husband_occupation = אדהה / אופה — likely 'baker' or related trade; husband_community = Jewish (ישראלית); husband_residence = נחני יה מצפה חיים (Mitzpe Chaim area, Mandatory Palestine); wife_name = Lea bat Shimon HaKohen Rapaport (לאה בת שמעון הכהן רפפורט); wife_age = 29; wife_occupation = Worker (פועלת); wife_community = Jewish

⚠ NOT OUR FAMILY (most likely). This certificate of divorce, issued 16 September 1941 in Petach Tikvah, Mandatory Palestine, dissolves the marriage of Lea bat Shimon HaKohen Rapaport (age 29) and Michael Sigal (age 29, baker). Lea's father is named Shimon HaKohen Rapaport — but our paternal-line Shimon was a Griffel ancestor, not a Rapaport, and our Lusia (Lea Weitzner-Rapaport) was hiding in Lwów under the alias 'Maria Cizlik' in 1941. This Lea Rapaport (b. ~1912) is therefore a different person, from a different Rapaport-HaKohen rabbinical branch. The CC-Cohen patronymic suggests she may still be a distant cousin within the broader Rapoport-Kohen rabbinical dynasty, but no proximate link to our family has been established.


## What is in this archive, and what is not — coverage audit, 11 August 2026

- Type: coverage_audit
- Source: This archive, audited against every source the family has sent.
- People: David (Memek) Rapaport, Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner), Shimon Rapaport, Dov Rapaport
- What it records: curated_people = 188; documents = 125; rapaport_tree = 1442 people, 177 with media, 917 files; griffel_tree = 390 people, 80 with media, 439 files; unidentified_photographs = 60; files_on_the_site = 1848; megabytes = 933; not_published_1 = Photobook2.pdf — all 47 of its pages are already here as individual photographs; not_published_2 = "Lea and Shimon .pdf" — the scan has never reached us

Every source the family has given us has been walked file by file and compared against what this site actually serves — in both directions, because either one alone lies. Nothing the site references is missing, and nothing we were given is unpublished, with exactly two exceptions, both named: Photobook2.pdf, whose 47 pages are already here one by one, and "Lea and Shimon .pdf", which has never reached us. Along the way this audit found 71 files that every earlier pass had skipped in silence — all of them PDFs, because each publisher filtered on image extensions and said nothing about what it dropped. Among them a 1938 document from Rebeka Griffel’s own folder and a 1942 list from the Bolechów Holzwerke, the town where Lusia was born. That is why the audit walks the sources and not the site: a check that only asks "does everything here work" cannot see what never arrived.


## Everyone named Rapaport in the Auschwitz victims database — 26 people, one of them ours

- Type: database_index
- Source: Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum, Database of victims, surname search "Rapaport". Read at source 6 August 2026.
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport
- Online: https://victims.auschwitz.org/en/victims?pattern=Rapaport
- What it records: total = 26; ours = 1

Twenty-six people named Rapaport appear in the Auschwitz-Birkenau Museum’s victims database. Exactly one is ours: Berisch, born 30 July 1886 at Radomyśl Wielki, prisoner 27310. The other twenty-five were born in Radom, Warsaw, Będzin, Lwów, Volove, Chocz, Karczowice, Oestringen, Podwołoczyska and Józefów, or have no birthplace recorded at all. Nothing connects them to this family except a surname that was common across Poland, and this archive has already been wrong once by reading a shared name as a shared line. They are listed here because they are worth listing — and kept out of the family’s own records for the same reason.

## Auschwitz transport 689 — 55 men from the prison in Tarnów, 27 March 1942

- Type: deportation_transport
- Source: Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum, Database of victims — transport 689. Read at source on the open web, 6 August 2026.
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport
- Online: https://victims.auschwitz.org/transports/689
- Online: https://victims.auschwitz.org/victims/188161

The transport that carried David’s father to Auschwitz, read from the Museum’s own database rather than from a copy. Fifty-five men from the prison in Tarnów, registered at Auschwitz on 27 March 1942 with numbers 27305 to 27359, sent by the Security Police (Sicherheitspolizei) and Security Service (Sicherheitsdienst) of the Kraków district. The Museum records that no documentation survives explaining the circumstances of the transport’s arrival and admission. Eighteen of the fifty-five were identified in the camp records as Jewish, and seventeen of those died at Auschwitz; at least forty-one of the fifty-five died there. Berish Rapaport held number 27310 — the sixth in the block — and died two days after arrival, on 29 March 1942. The detail that changes the story is where the transport started: the prison in Tarnów. The family came from Radomyśl Wielki, a short way from Tarnów, and their registered address in 1924 was Tarnów, Folwarczna 9. He was not taken from Przemyśl and sent east; he was held in the town his family belonged to.

## Lusia’s own Displaced Persons card, Brussels, 26 December 1945

- Type: dp_card
- Source: Basia, received 19 August 2026. Same series and provenance as David’s card: Commissariat Belge au Rapatriement, received by the BVM on 3 October 1969.
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner), David (Memek) Rapaport, Dov Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_19/lusia_dp_card_1945_front.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_19/lusia_dp_card_1945_back.jpg
- What it records: annotation_top_left = ép.se RAPAPORT — wife of Rapaport; family_name = WEITZNER; given_name = Léa; signature_of_registrant = Rapaport Lea; claimed_nationality = Polonaise; religion = Israélite; birthdate_as_written = 8.4.1916; birthplace_as_written = Haifa, Palestine — SHE WAS BORN IN BOLECHÓW; father = Elias; mother_maiden_name = Mathilde Weinreb; desired_destination = Palestine; last_residence_1_january_1938 = Lemberg (Lwów), Pologne; languages_in_order = German, Polish, Russian; prisoner_of_war = No

Lusia’s own card, the twin of David’s. She registered in Brussels on 26 December 1945 — three and a half months BEFORE he did on 9 April 1946, and eight months before Dov was born there. She signed it Rapaport Lea while the clerk filed her under her maiden name and added "ép.se Rapaport" at the top. Three things it gives us. Her parents: father Elias, mother’s maiden name MATHILDE WEINREB — the Weitzner–Weinreb line. The remark "Vient de Prague" — she came from a camp in Prague, which nothing else in this archive mentions and which is now an open question. And a birthplace that is simply wrong: HAIFA, PALESTINE. She was born in Bolechów. David’s card of the same series gives Cieszyn, also wrong. Two cards, two wrong birthplaces — and on Lusia’s the desired destination is Palestine. The archive records the pattern and asserts no motive.

> Open question: WHICH camp in Prague, and when was she there? Nothing else in this archive mentions Prague at all.

> Open question: Why is her birthplace given as Haifa? Her stated destination on the same card is Palestine. No source states a reason.

## Descendant chart of Abraham Abe Rapaport — Basia, 2026

- Type: family_tree_chart
- Source: Compiled by Basia from Tarnów and Kraków registers using Ahnenblatt 2.99p; plotted at 914 × 297 mm — one board of a tree that runs five metres. Received 5 August 2026.
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/abraham_abe_descendant_chart.pdf

THE CHART THAT NAMED MOSES SAUL’S PARENTS. Read 5 August 2026. It descends from Abraham Abe Rapaport and carries document thumbnails above most cards, so Basia is working from registers rather than from other people’s trees.

The card that matters: SZABSE RAPAPORT, born 30 January 1823 at Tarnów, died 1884, married to ITA FEIGA JAHR (died before 1897). On the sibling bar beneath them sits MOSES SAUL RAPAPORT, born about 1858, died 8 November 1933 at Tarnów, married to MENYCHA LEINER.

Every particular matches what this archive already held independently — the Tarnów death record 1933/152 gives the same death date, the same age and the same two parents, and we had Menukha’s maiden name Leiner from the Sambor marriage record of 1913, which Basia did not use. The chart adds two things we never had: Ita Feiga’s maiden name, and Szabse’s date of birth.

Also on that sibling bar, so also children of Szabse and Ita Feiga: LIEBE (b. 28 March 1847), MAJER (b. about 1849, d. 22 October 1920, married Cerka/Cecylia Margulies) and IZCHAK CHAIM (d. 15 August 1921, Kraków). Moses Saul had brothers and sisters, and this archive did not know their names an hour earlier.

The chart reaches further back still — Herschl Rapaport, born about 1792 at Tarnów, died 23 May 1874 — and outward across Tarnów and Kraków. It has NOT yet been read line by line.

## Basia’s GEDCOM — the whole Rapaport tree as data, 5 August 2026

- Type: family_tree_reference
- Source: Ahnenblatt 2.99p, GEDCOM 5.5.1, UTF-8, exported 5 August 2026 at 19:12. 1,442 individuals, 480 families, 31,716 lines, 1.1 MB. The file this archive asked for.
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/rapaport_from_abraham_2026-08-05.ged

THE FILE THAT LETS THE TWO TREES BE COMPARED BY MACHINE. Read 5 August 2026.

1,442 individuals against our 179 — eight times the size. Birth years run from 1784, Abraham Abe Rapaport, to 1988. Surnames beyond Rapaport (196): Weidenfeld 35, Bornstein 34, Turkel 32, Schneerson 31, Horowitz 17 — the rabbinical connections the Rosenstein sheets pointed at are inside her tree, not just alongside it.

THE TREES AGREE. 36 people match on an EXACT birth date, and on every one of those the death dates agree too, except where one of us simply had nothing. Two independent research efforts, one from Polish registers and one from family papers and Israeli records, produced the same answers. That is the strongest validation this archive has had.

ALL FOUR PRINCIPALS ARE IN IT: Dawid Mendel b.25.12.1911 d.29.8.1990; Lea (Lusia) Weitzner (Wizner) b.8.4.1913 d.1996; Shimon b.22.6.1937; Dov b.28.8.1946.

WHAT WE GAINED IMMEDIATELY: Jechiel Turkel — Alte Leja’s husband — died 17 JULY 1942 AT AUSCHWITZ. We had no death date for him at all. Inge Nussbaum died at Jerusalem, 14 January 2009, born at Kassel. Plus two birthplaces.

WHAT IS STILL TO DO: she holds 52 Rapaports with full birth dates that we do not, and 24 Rapaports whose death falls between 1939 and 1945 — Tarnów, Radomyśl Wielki, Kraków, Łuków, Biała Podlaska, Pińczów, Oświęcim. That is the family in the Holocaust, named. Importing them is a decision, not a formality: her confidence is not automatically ours, and anything brought across should carry her as its source.


## Jacob’s Griffel tree — David’s mother’s side, 390 people, received 10 August 2026

- Type: gedcom_tree
- Source: Jacob, Griffel family archive (Ahnenblatt export), received 10 August 2026 (4_Griffel.ged, 390 individuals, 140 families, with 569 image files)
- People: Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel), David (Memek) Rapaport, Lajzor Griffel, Sara Matel Chajes

A second family archive, and a different family. Not one of its 569 files appears in Basia’s Rapaport export — this is the GRIFFEL line, David’s mother’s people, the side of the house this archive had least on. Rebeka Griffel, of whom we held a name, a year and a death recorded only in Lusia’s memoir, appears here with her parents: Lajzor Griffel, born 26 March 1850 at Nadwórna, died 1918, and Sara Matel Chajes, born 5 March 1851, died 1940. She had twelve brothers and sisters. Where the two archives overlap they agree without having consulted each other: Berish born 1886 at Radomyśl Wielki and dead at Auschwitz in 1942; David born 1911 at Nadwórna and dead at Haifa in 1990; Lota born about 1915. Jacob dates Rebeka’s death "1942?" — with the question mark — which sits beside our 1942 and the 1943 on her Yad Vashem page. All three are kept.

## The Lurie Legacy — Descendants of the Shach, with Basia’s 2026 overlay

- Type: genealogical_paper
- Source: Dr Neil Rosenstein, "The Lurie Legacy: The House of Davidic Royal Descent" (2004), Appendix B, pages 342–343. Pink highlighting added by Basia in 2026, keyed "Individuals and connections confirmed in the Rapaport family tree 2026".
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/lurie_descendants_of_shach.pdf

Basia’s marks run our line up into a published rabbinical genealogy. Highlighted in pink and named on the sheet: MOSES SAUL, and IZCHAK MAJER CHAIM — the same brothers who stand beside Moses Saul on her own descendant chart. Above them she highlights SHABTAI, A.B.D. Dombrowa, died 1897, with an arrow to the Weidenfeld sheet; and higher still ABRAHAM ABALE, A.B.D. Tarnow, died 1872, the head of her chart. The ascent continues through Isaac Rappaport, A.B.D. Pinczow (1741-1774), to SHABTAI HaKOHEN — the SHaCH, 1621-1662 — and across to Saul WAHL and Israel KATZENELLENBOGEN.

HOW THIS ARCHIVE TREATS IT. The documented line stops at Szabse and Ita Feiga. Everything above them is a published compilation, and the Davidic descent at the top is TRADITION — held exactly as the Rapaport-Kohen priestly lineage is held: recorded, attributed, and never stated as proven descent. It is kept deliberately apart from the part we can put a record behind, so the documented part cannot be weakened by association if the traditional part is ever challenged.

NOTE THE CONFLICT: Rosenstein’s Shabtai died 1897, Basia’s own chart has Szabse dying 1884. See h_szabse_death_year.


## The Lurie Legacy — the Weidenfeld family, with Basia’s 2026 overlay

- Type: genealogical_paper
- Source: Dr Neil Rosenstein, "The Lurie Legacy" (2004), Appendix B, page 346. Pink highlighting by Basia, 2026.
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/lurie_weidenfeld_family.pdf

Headed by SHABTAI RAPPAPORT, A.B.D. Dombrowa, died 1897 — the man Basia marks as our ancestor. Almost the whole descent below him is highlighted as confirmed against the Rapaport tree of 2026: his daughter Rachel (d. 1914), who married Jacob WEIDENFELD (1840-1894), A.B.D. Grzymalow, author of the responsa "Kochav MiYaakov"; Meir, A.B.D. Lukow (d. 1920); Isaac Haim, A.B.D. Ostrowiec Swietokrzyski (d. 1921), who married a daughter of Zeev Nachum BORNSTEIN — the card Basia labels MARGULESA; and onward to Nachum (1874-1939), the Chazon Nachum, A.B.D. Dombrowa, who "perished with his family", and to DovBerish of Trzebin (1881-1965), the Chebiner Rov.

If the identification holds, our paternal line runs into a dynasty of rabbinical court heads across Galicia — and into the Holocaust, repeatedly and by name. IT DEPENDS ENTIRELY on whether the 1897 Shabtai is the 1884 Szabse. Unresolved; see h_szabse_death_year.

Grunfeld Josef b.31.III.1902

Registration cards of the Jewish Community of Lemberg — Lwów — from the years the ghetto was administered. One of the four is Leibisch Griffel, Rebeka’s brother and David’s uncle by marriage: born 16 May 1885, living at Sommerstein 17/8, working for the District Post Administration, registration card number 17262. It places him in Lwów, in work, inside the system that was counting Jews. His Yad Vashem record gives his death only as "Shoah", with no year. This card is the last document that shows him alive.
