A Griffel who got out. Edward, born in Nadwórna, thirty-eight years old, an exporter, crossed into the United States from Montreal on 26 May 1941 to reside permanently, giving his father David Griffel at Kolomyja as his nearest relative in the country he came from. Doron reports that Basia has now finished the Weitzner side and found many of them emigrated to the United States, settling mainly in New Jersey; this card is the same movement on the Griffel side, and it is dated eight months before the family in Galicia was destroyed.


## Zygmunt Griffel — Sejm Album industrial feature, 1934/35

- Type: industry_press
- Source: ALBUM-SKOROWIDZ SENATU I SEJMU RZPLITEJ ORAZ SEJMU ŚLĄSKIEGO, page 110* (industrial sponsor section). Polish governmental industrial-directory yearbook.
- People: Zygmunt Griffel, David Mendel Griffel Sr., Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel), David (Memek) Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/IMG-20260520-WA0018.jpg
- What it records: company = ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL — Tartaki parowe, Przemysł drzewny, Eksport drzewa (Steam sawmills, timber industry, timber export); headquarters = Lwów, ul. Kopernika 5 (additional address ul. Kopernika 55); founded = 1923; infrastructure = Timber storage facilities at Gdynia and Gdańsk Baltic ports; main_product = Softwood (sztriad) lumber — 96.7% of output; 1934_35_exports = 60,000 tons to England, worth >5 million złotych; export_distribution = England 82.8%, Holland 8.3%, Germany 2.9%, Switzerland 2.3%, France 1.9%, other 1.7%; branding = Polish timber sold via English-market agents previously known as 'drzewo gdańskie' (Gdańsk wood) — Zygmunt Griffel attempting to establish 'drzewo polskie' branding; photo = Headshot of P. Zygm. Griffel; photo of timber being loaded onto ship ('Ładowanie drzewa na statek')

Zygmunt Griffel was David Memek's first cousin once removed — son of Dawid Mendel Griffel Sr. (Rebeka's brother, Leizor Griffel's eldest son). This page establishes him as a major Galician-Jewish timber industrialist in 1934-35: 60,000 t/year to England, a five-port operation, with ambitions to brand Polish softwood. The 1938 Muszyna sawmill operating under 'Segal-Żupnik-Wilf' (per Rafał Żebrowski's Holocaust article) is identified in 1938 Przegląd Drzewny as a Zygmunt Griffel front company. Lusia's Muszyna hotel Willa Helin was in Zygmunt's sawmill town — the family-business ecosystem extended from Lwów-Gdynia exports down to Carpathian spa-town milling.


## Zygmunt Griffel — Przegląd Drzewny press feature, 1938 ('Export sets to Palestine')

- Type: industry_press
- Source: Przegląd Drzewny (Polish Timber Review), 1938 issue. Found and cited by Basia 21 May 2026.
- People: Zygmunt Griffel
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/Zygmunt Griffel 1938.png

Article 'Eksport kompletów do Palestyny' (Export of sets to Palestine) listing Galician-Polish timber firms exporting to the British Mandate. Identifies 'Zygmunt Griffel Lwów (Segal, Żupnik, i Wilf)' — confirming the Muszyna sawmill operating under the holding-name 'Segal-Żupnik-Wilf' (per Żebrowski's Holocaust article) was Zygmunt Griffel's company. This is the key citation that linked Lusia's Muszyna hotel to a Griffel-family timber operation.

## The Story of Lusia (Sipura shel Lusia)

- Type: memoir
- Source: Family — scanned PDF uploaded by Dalia, Dana, Doron and Daniel 20 May 2026. 90 pages, Hebrew, with embedded photos and dedication page. Compiled in 1986 by Esther Weiss based on Lusia Rapaport's first-person recollections, with extensive multi-session interviews recorded by Esther's pen and later edited by Moshe Goter.
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner), David (Memek) Rapaport, Shimon Rapaport, Dov Rapaport, Lota (Lotte) Schmid-Rapaport, Feige / Tzipora Weitzner, Moses (Mojzesz) Weitzner, Pnina Weitzner
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/lusia_memoir.pdf

Lusia (Leah) Rapaport née Weitzner's memoir, dictated and compiled in 1986 by her granddaughter's teacher Esther Weiss. The primary historical document for Lusia's life from childhood in Bolechów through marriage in Muszyna (Feb 1938), survival under Nazi occupation in Lwów with false identities, escape with David and Shimon, post-war Brussels and the journey to Israel. Settled in Haifa, Moriah Street 93. OCR'd 2026-05-20 via Google Cloud Vision (~70k Hebrew chars). Translated to English and Polish by Claude Haiku 4.5 (batched). PRIMARY SOURCE for resolving H1 Mosina = Muszyna, and confirming David's wooden-wagon escape, Lota's existence, the Legionów 24 address, and the marriage date Feb 1938.


## How to read Basia’s marked-up boards — pink means matched, and a blank is deliberate

- Type: method_note
- Source: Basia, 20 August 2026
- People: Szabse (Shabtai) Rapaport

Stated by Basia on 20 August 2026, and it changes how every board she has sent should be read: on the Rosenstein sheets she marks in pink ONLY the elements that match the records she has verified. So she did not mark Shabtai’s death date — she left the 1897 out on purpose, because she does not accept it. An unmarked element on one of her boards is not something she overlooked. It is something she is declining to confirm.


## Notary’s day-book, Tarnów, 19 October 1904 — Mojżesz Rapaport gives his wife Menicha power of attorney to collect the post

- Type: notarial_record
- Source: Basia, 19-20 August 2026 — Tarnów notarial records; sent as a Word file with the scan embedded, transcribed and translated by Basia
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/tarnow_notary_1904_postal_poa.png
- What it records: entry_number = 392; date = 19 October 1904; office = Deputy of the Imperial-Royal notary in Tarnów, by decree of the Imperial-Royal District Court in Tarnów of 25 July 1904; party = Mojżesz Rapaport, kapitalista (capitalist) in Tarnów; act = Legalizacya podpisu na pełnomocnictwie — legalisation of the signature on a power of attorney dated Tarnów, 19 October; granted_to = his wife, Menicha Rapaportowa (Menycha née Leiner); purpose = do odbioru przesyłek pocztowych — to collect postal items; fee = 2 korony

Nineteen years before the century turned violent, a man walked into a notary’s office in Tarnów and paid two crowns so that his wife could pick up his mail. The clerk wrote entry 392: Mojżesz Rapaport, capitalist in Tarnów, legalises his signature on a power of attorney for his wife Menicha to receive postal items. That is the whole document, and it is worth more than its size. This is Moses Saul Rapaport, born 1858, died in Tarnów on 8 November 1933 — David’s grandfather, Dov’s great-grandfather — and the tree records his occupation as "merchant"; the notary wrote "capitalist". And it is the only document this archive holds that names his wife at all: Menycha Leiner has, until today, been a name on a tree with no birth date, no death date and no paper of her own. Here she is, in 1904, being given legal authority over the family post.

> Open question: Why did Moses Saul need a notary for this at all — was he travelling, ill, or simply away on business? The entry does not say.


## Yad Vashem Page of Testimony — Bernard Dov Rapaport (filed 1953)

- Type: page_of_testimony
- Source: Yad Vashem Central Database of Shoah Victims' Names, record file 90394
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport, Moses (Moshe) Saul Rapaport, Menukha Rapaport, David (Memek) Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/Bernard Dov Rapaport.pdf
- What it records: name = Bernard Dov Rapaport; year_of_birth = 1888; place_of_birth = Radomyśl, Poland; father = Moshe; mother = Menukha; occupation = merchant; pre_war_residence = Tarnów, Poland; during_war = Przemyśl, Poland; place_and_year_of_death = Auschwitz, 1940; submitter = family member, 1953; page_number = 53072; subject = Bernard (Berisz) Rapaport; submitter_residence = Haifa; submitter_address = street only partly legible; house number 93

Primary documentary confirmation of Berisz's parents (Moshe + Menukha) and Holocaust fate (Auschwitz). PoT year of birth (1888) differs from passport application (1886) — the passport is a contemporary primary document and is the more reliable source for date. WHO FILED IT. The declaration at the foot gives a filer living in Haifa, at a street only partly legible with house number 93, signing on 26 April 1953. Where the form asks what the filer was to the person, the word written is בנו — his son. The signature reads רפפורט ד. The identification does not rest on that signature: Berish and Rebeka had one son, David, their other child being Lota, so "his son" is David whoever wrote the letters. He filed it seven years after reaching the country, for parents he had last seen before the war. Yad Vashem’s own database would show the submitter independently; it refused connection from this machine on 11 August 2026, so that confirmation is still outstanding.

> Open question: Confirm the submitter name against Yad Vashem’s own name database — it refused connection on 11 August 2026 and has not been checked.

## ‚Świadectwo kwalifikacyjne’ (Qualification Certificate), Stanisławów, 29 July 1924 — identification REOPENED 6 August 2026

- Type: passport_application
- Source: Polish Galician archives — located 23 May 2026 by Basia. WA0001 = application form. WA0002 = passport photograph.
- People: Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport, David (Memek) Rapaport, Lota (Lotte) Schmid-Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_swiadectwo_1924_p1.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_swiadectwo_1924_p2.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_swiadectwo_1924_p3.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_swiadectwo_1924_p4.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_swiadectwo_1924_p5.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/IMG-20260523-WA0001.jpg
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/IMG-20260523-WA0002.jpg
- What it records: applicant_name = Rapaport Berisch; father_name = Rapaport Mojżesz Saul; origin_address = Tarnów, Folwarczna 9 (today Waryńskiego St.); current_address = Berisch Rapaport, Stanisławów Kościuszki 4; residence_last_6_months = Stanisławów Kościuszki 4; personal_doc_number = L 19376/22 (issued by Starostwo / District Office); municipal_registry = Dąbrowa; physical_description = average height, elongated face, black hair, black eyes, proportionate nose and mouth; children_under_14 = Mendel 12 lat · Lotte 8 lat; destination = Wiedeń (Vienna); police_clearance = No obstacles to issuing a foreign passport; certificate_number = 93; date_issued = 29/VII 1924; applicant = Rapaport Berisch

This record was closed on 25 May 2026 as belonging to a different man, and its people were removed. That verdict rested on the second file in it — a Tarnów birth register page recording a Benzion Rapaport born 6 August 1884 to Mojżesz Rapaport and Regina Schiff. That page is indeed someone else. But it is not this certificate. The certificate, read in full from Basia’s own scan on 6 August 2026, says: applicant Rapaport Berisch; father Mojżesz Saul; origin Tarnów, Folwarczna 9; present address Stanisławów, Kościuszki 4; and, under children aged fourteen and under, „Mendel 12 lat, Lotte 8 lat”. David Mendel was twelve in July 1924 and Lota was eight. Basia has now filed this certificate inside our Berish’s own folder. Four things point one way — the father’s name, the Stanisławów address the archive already held for him, the two children by name and age, and a date one month before his August 1924 passport application. The 1884 Tarnów birth entry points the other way and stays on the record. The question is open again rather than settled the other way, and it is one for Basia.

> Open question: For Basia: the certificate names Mendel 12 and Lotte 8 and gives Kościuszki 4 — our Berish. What is the 1884 Tarnów birth entry for Benzion doing in the same file?

## Dov’s journeys back — Warsaw, Przemyśl, Brussels, Katowice (2015–2019)

- Type: photograph_album
- Source: Four shared Google Photos albums supplied by Doron Rapaport, 11 August 2026.
- People: Dov Rapaport, David (Memek) Rapaport, Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport, Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel)
- What it records: warsaw = 14–20 April 2015; przemysl = 19 April 2015; brussels = 21–23 June 2018; katowice = 17–19 January 2019; published = 120; reviewed = every frame, 11 August 2026; excluded = 1 — a hotel lavatory

Four albums, 121 frames, 120 published. They are not holidays. Przemyśl is where Berish and Rebeka were when the war came — the camp register gives it as Berish’s residence, and the memoir says the Germans killed one of his parents there. Brussels is where Dov was born in 1946. Katowice is the town of the separation in chapter 5. Warsaw is the city that was flattened and rebuilt. Dov walked and drove them with his son and photographed what he saw. Every frame was looked at before publishing; one was left out, a hotel lavatory, and saying which is part of the check.


## The Griffels in the Galician press, 1866-1946 — 128 pages, 142 notices

- Type: press_collection
- Source: Basia — located, transcribed and compiled by her from Gazeta Lwowska, Chwila, Kurier Stanisławowski, Nowy Dziennik, Nasz Głos, Przegląd Drzewny and some thirty other titles; sent 23 August 2026. Translated into English and Hebrew for this archive.
- People: David (Memek) Rapaport, Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport, Dr Jacob Griffel
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/griffel_press_notices_1866_1946.pdf
- What it records: span = 1866-1946, eighty years; notices = 142, every one translated in full into English and Hebrew with the Polish or German original beside it; read_on_the_site = https://rapaportfamily.github.io/#/press; earliest = Gazeta Lwowska, 31 July 1866 — Griffel Benjamin, Griffel Chaim Samuel and Griffel Taube of Nadwórna give to the Israelite Patriotic Central Committee’s war fund during the Austro-Prussian War; latest = Rzeszów voivodeship gazette, 21 November 1946 — the steam sawmill and box factory at Zarszyn, belonging to Zygmunt Griffel of Lwów, passes to the communist state; our_own_line = Berish Rapaport appears twice: in the register entry of 31 October 1912 he is given procuration in the Griffel-Schwarz ironworks lease at Pasieczna, and in June 1913 the drilling journal Ropa reports a new oil company at Pasieczna called "Griffel jun. et Rappaport"; the_four_brothers = The trial reports of 1935 name Eisig Chaim Griffel’s sons and their jobs: Szulim in the Stanisławów head office, JAKÓB running the Korneuburg refinery in Austria, Mendel on the technical side, Ozjasz Henryk in his father’s office. That is the sibling group on the wartime rescue list.; the_three_engagements = Chwila announces Jakób Griffel to Fela FREILICH (1924), Mendel Griffel to Lucja AJCHENBAUM of Lublin (1925), and Szalom Griffel to Rega FORTGANG (1926) — all three surnames appear on the rescue list, and Rega answers the "Regi…" torn off its right edge.

Basia wrote that while researching Muszyna she came across an unprecedented number of press sources, and that the local papers described the Griffels’ ups and downs much as papers describe celebrities now — so that in the articles you can catch glimpses of their personalities, which is rare. She is right. Eighty years of them: Taube Griffel’s spice, haberdashery and iron shop at Nadwórna registered in 1882 with power of attorney to her son Leizor; the credit cooperative of 1890; Leizor on the jury list of 1892; the first petroleum refinery at Nadwórna in 1900; the sawmill; the ironworks lease at Pasieczna in 1912 where Berish Rapaport is given the signature; the great fire of 1907 with Leizor collecting for the homeless; the 2,500 wagons a year and the exports as far as China; the refinery fire of 1923; Eisig Chaim elected fifth to the Stanisławów kahal board in 1924 with 2,175 votes; three sons’ engagements announced by their own clerks; the strike of 1926; the anonymous letter with a skull; and then July 1930, when it all came down — eleven million złoty of debt, widows and orphans who had trusted the firm more than the banks, the family leaving town days before the filing, Eisig Chaim dead in a Kraków prison that October, a five-year composition in which the administrators collected over a million and a half and the creditors got about a hundred thousand, workers unpaid for twenty-three months, two brothers beaten in the street outside the courthouse, and an acquittal in February 1936. And alongside all of it the other brother’s house holding: Zygmunt Griffel of Lwów, 60,000 tonnes of timber to England in one year, orange crates to Palestine, boycott sheets naming him as a Jew not to be traded with, ten thousand złoty given to Poland’s air-defence loan in March 1939, and the state taking his sawmill in 1946.

## Typed list of fifteen families, addressed care of Jakob Griffel in Bucharest — identified by the Pilecki Institute as belonging to the wartime operation to issue foreign passports to Jews

- Type: rescue_list
- Source: Jacob, Griffel family archive, received 10 August 2026. Identified by Ms Manowska of the Pilecki Institute, reported by Basia on 19 August 2026.
- People: David (Memek) Rapaport, Dr Jacob Griffel
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/griffel_2026_08/photos/I16__jacob_griffel.jpg
- What it records: language = German, typewritten, torn along the right edge — the right-hand third of every line is missing; entries = 15 numbered, a pencilled "16 =" below; marked_with_X = entries 6, 8 and 9 carry a pencilled X. Meaning unknown.; forwarding_address = Jakob Griffel, c/o Ing. L. Filderman, Strada Paleolog[u], Bucureşti; who_jacob_griffel_was = Dr Jacob Griffel (1900-1962), the Agudath Israel and Vaad Hatzalah rescue representative in Istanbul — the channel through which Orthodox rescue money and papers moved into occupied Europe; who_filderman_was = the address places the sheet with the Bucharest end of that channel; Wilhelm Filderman led Romanian Jewry, and the pencil reads "Ing. L. Filderman"

A torn sheet of typing that this archive misfiled for ten days as a portrait of Dr Jacob Griffel, because the file was named after him. It is not a portrait. It is a list of fifteen families — names, birth dates, street addresses in Kraków, Sosnowiec, Bielsko and Lublin — with his own forwarding address pencilled at the foot: care of Ing. L. Filderman, Strada Paleologu, Bucharest. Jacob Griffel was the Agudath Israel and Vaad Hatzalah rescue man in Istanbul. Shown this sheet, Ms Manowska of the Pilecki Institute — who researches the "passports of life" — recognised at sight that it came from the operation that issued foreign passports to Jews, and asked immediately where it had been found and what its reference number was.

Six of the fifteen entries are the children of Isaac Chaim Griffel and Judith Breit, and the birth dates match this family’s tree to the month: Dawid Mendel b. 1902 with his wife Laja and their daughter Rebeka Rachela; Schalom b. 1904 with his wife and two little girls; Samuel b. 1913; Amalia b. 1912; Rebeka b. July 1919; Osias Heinrich b. 1910. Entry 15 is their sister Teofila with her husband Rosenbojm and their small son. This was a man in Bucharest trying to get his own family onto paper that might save them.

And then the outcome, which the film must not soften. Samuel lived, and died in Brooklyn in 1999. Osias Heinrich lived, and died in Jersey City in 1968. Menashe Karmel, entry 6, lived until 1989. Schalom, his wife and both daughters were murdered in 1942. Schmul Rosenbojm was murdered in Lublin in 1942 and his son Izajasz in 1943. Zalman Horowitz died in 1945. Whether any passport was ever issued against this sheet, and whether it reached anybody, this archive cannot say.

> Open question: What is the reference number and provenance of this sheet? Ms Manowska asked first and it is still unanswered — Jacob sent the file on 10 August with the rest of the Griffel library.

> Open question: Who put a pencilled X beside entries 6, 8 and 9 — and did it mean saved, refused, or already dead?

> Open question: The last line ends with a dash and the word "Eisig". Chaim Eiss of Zurich was one of the six men of the Ładoś Group who made these passports; "Eisig Chaim" is also a given name that runs through this very family. It could be either. Do not decide it in a film.

> Open question: Are Minna and Toni at entry 11 the same two girls the tree calls Chaja Mindel and Thea? Both were murdered in 1942 either way.


## Basia closes the research phase — 24 August 2026

- Type: research_note
- Source: Basia, WhatsApp, 24 August 2026
- What it records: she_is_from = POZNAŃ, in western Poland — not Kraków. The About page said Kraków in all four languages and has been corrected.; pilecki_meeting = She spoke with Ms Manowska and they arranged to meet on Wednesday at about 2 p.m.; Ms Manowska will place the call.; she_read_the_site = She has been following the work and says "Start Here" is very good.; still_available_if_wanted = More Griffel material from the interwar press exists, and the GERMAN-LANGUAGE press has not been searched at all.

On 24 August 2026 Basia ended the research phase in her own words: "This is the end of this project, or project phase, and I won’t add anything more, because now you need to master the information that has already been collected and the threads that have emerged."

It is worth recording as a finding rather than as admin. A year of work by somebody who reads Polish registers for a living has produced more than this family can yet hold in its head, and she is saying the useful thing next is not more paper but understanding the paper we have. The archive as it stands — 138 documents, 142 press notices, two large trees, ninety pages of memoir — is the result.

She left one door open, and it is written down so it does not get lost: more Griffel material from the interwar press is available if the family wants it, and the GERMAN-LANGUAGE press has not been looked at at all. Given that the family’s businesses reached Vienna, Korneuburg, Danzig and Bucharest, that is not a small gap — it is simply one nobody has opened yet.

She also asked for a correction, which is the reason this note exists at all: she is from POZNAŃ, not Kraków. The About page had said Kraków in all four languages. A wrong fact about a living person who gave us her work is worse than a missing one, and it is now right in all four.


## NKVD repressions against residents of the border zone, 1939-1941 (IPN research paper 6066)

- Type: research_paper
- Source: Polish Institute of National Remembrance (IPN), shared by Basia 17 May 2026 via WhatsApp
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/represje-nkwd-wobec-mieszkancow-strefy-przygranicznej-1939-1941_6066.pdf

IPN academic paper covering Soviet repressions in the border zone with Germany 1939-1941. Includes a detailed section on 'Passportization' — the forced Soviet passport campaign that all residents of border towns like Przemyśl had to navigate. Basia shared this paper as historical CONTEXT — Leah was briefly in Soviet-occupied Przemyśl in 1939, and this paper documents what life under that occupation looked like for everyone living there. The paper also informs a planned formal IPN application for any Rapaport family records held by the Polish Institute of National Remembrance (see h_ipn_application). Reading this paper helps the family understand the conditions David and Leah lived through — both Soviet and Nazi occupations — before they were able to escape and reach Brussels in 1946.

## Request for temporary residence in Belgium, 28 December 1945

- Type: residence_application
- Source: Basia, received 19 August 2026. Transcribed and translated by Basia.
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_19/lusia_residence_request_1945-12-28.pdf
- What it records: body = International Refugee Committee, Belgian Branch; submitted_by = the association "Aid to Jewish Victims of War"; on_behalf_of = Lea Weitzner, born Haifa 8.04.1916, Polish; residence_abroad = Lwów; address_in_belgium = 250 rue des Coteaux, Schaerbeek; reason_for_stay = came from a camp in Prague, awaiting departure for Palestine; duration_requested = 6 months; dated = Brussels, 28 December 1945

Two days after she registered as a displaced person, a Jewish aid society asked the Belgian authorities to let her stay six months. It repeats the two strange things from her card — born Haifa, came from a camp in Prague — and adds an address: 250 rue des Coteaux, Schaerbeek. That is where Lusia was living in Brussels, and where she was still living when Dov was born eight months later.


## Belgian Ministry of Justice — permission to stay pending emigration, 7 February 1946

- Type: residence_permit
- Source: Basia, received 19 August 2026
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_19/lusia_stay_permit_1946-02-07.pdf
- What it records: ministry = Ministère de la Justice, Administration de la Police des Étrangers; reference = No. 2063253; dated = Bruxelles, 7.2.1946; addressed_to = the Chief Commissioner of Police, Schaerbeek; under_cover_of = Comité Intergouvernemental pour réfugiés; authorisation = authorised to stay in Belgium pending emigration; certificate = MODÈLE C, bearing the mention "EN VUE D’ÉMIGRATION"; validity = three months, renewable for two further periods of three months

The state answering. Six weeks after the aid society asked, the Ministry of Justice told the Schaerbeek police to enter her in the aliens’ register and issue a Modèle C certificate marked EN VUE D’ÉMIGRATION — permission to be in Belgium only while waiting to leave it. Three months at a time, twice renewable. Dov was born under this arrangement.

- Type: school_record

Sprawozdanie Dyrekcji I. Gimnazjum Państwowego im. M. Romanowskiego w Stanisławowie za rok szkolny 1927/28 — the annual report of the First State Gymnasium named for Mieczysław Romanowski, printed in Stanisławów in 1928 by Leon Dankiewicz, the same printer whose name sits at the foot of Berish’s 1924 certificate. It marks the school’s bicentenary, 1728–1928. This is where David was a pupil: the family had been in Stanisławów since 1918 and lived at Kościuszki 4. One of the three files Basia sent is labelled nieukończone — unfinished — and the archive repeats that label rather than guessing what it refers to.


## David in the school register — printed student lists of the First State Gymnasium, Stanisławów, 1925/26 to 1928/29

- Type: school_record
- Source: Basia, 23 August 2026 — annual reports of the First State Gymnasium named for M. Romanowski, Stanisławów
- People: David (Memek) Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/gimnazjum_student_list_1925_26.png
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/gimnazjum_student_list_1926_27.png
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_20/gimnazjum_student_list_1927_28.png
- What it records: 1925_26 = KLASA IV (39), group D — "Rapaport Dawid".; 1926_27 = KLASA IVa (29), group a — "Rapaport MENDEL DAWID", under both his given names.; 1927_28 = KLASA Vb (27), group d — "Rapaport Dawid".; 1928_29 = Not present. Basia’s covering note reads "Dawid Rapaport is not on the list of students", and it follows the 1928/29 heading.; also_on_these_pages = KLASA V of 1926/27 carries "Finder false Rapaport Chaim" and KLASA VI of 1927/28 "Finder f. Rapaport Chaim" — a different person, recorded here so nobody later mistakes him for ours.

Since 6 August this archive has said that the First State Gymnasium in Stanisławów is "where David was a pupil". It held the school’s annual reports, but not the student lists, so the statement was an inference. The lists are now here, and they carry his name three years running: Klasa IV in 1925/26, Klasa IVa in 1926/27 written out as "Rapaport Mendel Dawid", and Klasa Vb in 1927/28. He was born on 25 December 1911, so that is ages thirteen to sixteen. What was an assumption is now a printed record.

He is not on the 1928/29 list, and that is what Basia’s note refers to. Why he left after Klasa V — a change of school, a move, the family’s circumstances, or something else — no source here says. It is written down as a question rather than guessed at, and Basia has been asked to confirm that the sentence was meant about that one year.


## Arolsen tracing index card — Lea Rapaport née Weitzner, document pulled 17 December 1970

- Type: tracing_index_card
- Source: Arolsen Archives (formerly the International Tracing Service), DocID 68746393. Found online by Basia some months ago and sent on 24 August 2026, once Lusia’s own DP card made sense of it.
- People: Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner), Elias (Eliyahu) Weitzner, Matel (Mathilde) Weitzner (née Weinreb)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08_24/arolsen_index_card_lea_rapaport_1970.png
- What it records: name = RAPAPORT; maiden_name = Weißner — the German spelling of WEITZNER; given_name = Lea; geb_datum_field_contains = Haifa  (the PLACE, written in the DATE box); geb_ort_field_contains = 8.4.1916  (the DATE, written in the PLACE box); father = Elias; mother = Mathilde; reference = T/D 442582; docid = 68746393; entnommen_am = 17.12.1970 — "taken out on", with a clerk’s initials; ticked_on_the_list = DP Karten — and only that one; left_unticked = IRO Identity Card, IRO Case Card, Fiche Individuelle, Italienische Karte, Schwabing-Karte, AJDC-Belsen, CCLJ-Karte

An index card from the tracing service at Arolsen. It is not a record of Lusia’s life; it is a record of somebody LOOKING FOR IT. The card names her exactly as her own Displaced Persons card does — Lea Rapaport, born Weitzner, 8 April 1916, father Elias, mother Mathilde — which is what finally convinced Basia the two belonged together.

Two things on it are worth more than the rest.

FIRST, THE FIELDS ARE SWAPPED. "Geb.-Datum" — date of birth — contains the word Haifa. "Geb.-Ort" — place of birth — contains 8.4.1916. A clerk has put the place in the date box and the date in the place box. This archive has carried an open question about why Lusia’s DP card says she was born in Haifa when she was born in Bolechów. This is a second document carrying the same Haifa, and the first evidence that the two fields have been mishandled by somebody along the way. It does not settle the question. It changes what kind of question it is.

SECOND, ONLY ONE BOX IS TICKED. The card lists the sorts of record the file might contain — IRO identity card, IRO case card, Fiche Individuelle, an Italian card, a Schwabing card, an AJDC Belsen card, a CCLJ card. Only DP Karten is marked. So as far as this card goes, the DP card is the only thing the tracing service held for her, and she was not in the Belsen or Italian files. A blank with a reason is worth keeping.

"Entnommen am 17.12.1970" means the document was taken out of the file on 17 December 1970. Files were pulled when somebody asked for them. Nobody in this family knows who asked, or why, twenty-five years after the war.


## Jenta Rapaport registered at Rue Lairesse 88, Liège, during the war

- Type: wartime_registration
- Source: Wartime name record 4494611, box 15, internal number 110381. Received from Basia 6 August 2026.
- People: Jente Rapaport, Szewa Horowitz (m. Türkel), David (Memek) Rapaport
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/jente_rapaport_liege_wartime_record.png
- What it records: primary_name = Jenta RAPAPORT; date_of_birth = Oct 7, 1887; place_during_war = Liège; wartime_address = Rue Lairesse, 88; nationality = Polonaise (Polish); box_number = 15; internal_number = 110381

Jente Rapaport — David’s aunt, sister of his father Berish — spent the war in Liège at Rue Lairesse 88, registered as a Polish national. Her daughter Szewa Horowitz married Markus Türkel in Liège in 1941, and the archive already holds that marriage. Together they change the shape of one thing: when David and Lusia reached Belgium in 1946, Belgium was not a country the family had never been to. Part of it had been living there through the occupation, in a city 100 km from Brussels. Whether the two facts are connected — whether David went where he had people — is not stated by any document here, and the archive does not assert it.

> Open question: Did David and Lusia know Jente and Szewa were in Liège when they came to Belgium in 1946? Nothing in hand says so either way.


## Yad Vashem Page of Testimony 53075 — Lotte Schmid-Rapaport, filed by her brother David, 26 April 1953

- Type: yad_vashem_page_of_testimony
- Source: Yad Vashem, Page of Testimony No. 53075. Copy from Basia, Ahnenblatt family archive, received 6 August 2026; read 11 August 2026.
- People: Lota (Lotte) Schmid-Rapaport, David (Memek) Rapaport, Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport, Rebeka / Regina Rapaport (née Griffel)
- Scan: https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/yad_vashem/I15__yad_vashem_lotte_rapaport.jpg
- What it records: page_number = 53075; family_name = שמיד - רפפורט / Schmid – Rapaport; first_name = לוטה / Lotte; father = בעריש (Berish); mother = רבקה (Rebeka); marital_status = נשואה — married; date_of_birth = 1914; place_of_birth = וינה, אוסטריה — Vienna, Austria; nationality_1939 = פולנית — Polish; place_of_death = לבוב — Lwów; date_of_death = not filled in; submitted_by = her brother — אחיה — living in Haifa, house number 93; signed = 26.4.53; not_transcribed = the occupation line and the community line are not legible enough in this scan to transcribe, and are left blank rather than guessed

The third form David filled in that day. His sister Lotte was born in Vienna in 1914 — this page says 1914, the family trees say 27 September 1915, and both are kept — daughter of Berish and Rebeka, married, a Polish national in 1939, and she died at Lwów. This archive held no death for her at all: she was a birth record from Vienna and then nothing. Her brother, writing in Haifa eight years after the war, put down where she died and signed himself אחיה, her brother. He did not fill in the year. The married name on the page, Schmid, is new here too — nothing else in this archive records that she married, or whom. CROSS-CHECKED 11 August 2026 against both family archives. Basia holds her as Charlotta Rapaport, born 27 September 1915 in Vienna, married to "… Szmid" — the surname without a given name — and records NO death. Jacob holds her as Lota Rapaport, born about 1915, with no marriage and no death. So the married name is confirmed twice over and the place of death is confirmed nowhere else: this page is the only statement about her fate in any of the three archives. Note also that Basia’s tree contains a first cousin, Charlotte Horowitz, born 21 November 1914 in Vienna to Berish’s sister Jente — same name, same city, one year apart. The 1914 on this page matches the cousin; the parents on this page, בעריש and רבקה, do not. The parents decide it.

> Open question: Who was Schmid? Nothing else here records Lotte’s marriage. And the year of her death is blank on the page — Lwów is where, not when.

> Open question: Szmid’s given name is unknown in both family trees and on the page. And no source but this page says anything about how Lotte died.

# Timeline (45 events)

- **1888** — Rebeka Griffel born in Nadwórna: Daughter of Leizor Griffel and Sara née Chajes, merchants of Nadwórna.
- **1905** — Elias Weitzner and Matel Weinreb marry in Dolina: Statutory civil marriage in Dolina, Galicia. From Lea's birth certificate.
- **1911** — Feige (Tzipora) Weitzner born in Bolechów: Leah's older sister. Father Elias is listed as a cattle dealer.
- **1911-12-25** — David (Dawid Mendel) Rapaport born in Nadwórna: Son of Berisz Rapaport and Rebeka Griffel. The verified birthplace — the Brussels DP card listing 'Cieszyn (Tesin)' was a transcription error by the French clerk.
- **1912-07-25** — Regina Rapaport registers at Alfredówka in Krynica: Regina (Rebeka) Rapaport, wife of a merchant from Nadwórna, registers alone at the Alfredówka guesthouse in the Krynica resort on 25 July 1912 (newspaper page 7).
- **1913-04-08** — Leah Weitzner born in Bolechów: Daughter of Elias (cattle dealer) and Matel (née Weinreb) Weitzner. Named in Bolechów synagogue on 12 April. Family death certificate lists 6.4.1916 — a conflict to resolve.
- **1916** — Moses (Mojzesz) Weitzner born in Bolechów: Leah's younger brother. Father Elias listed again as cattle dealer.
- **1933** — Weitzner family emigrates to Mandatory Palestine: Elias and Matel sell the tannery and emigrate with three of their four children. Elias obtains a British 'capitalist' visa with 1000 GBP. Leah does NOT join them — she remains in Galicia.
- **1935-02-10** — David Rapaport and Leah Weitzner marry in Bolechów: The civil record gives Bolechów, 10 February 1935 — Lusia 21, David 23. Her memoir places the wedding in Muszyna in February 1938, three years later and in another town; written forty years afterwards, it remembers the marriage as the beginning of the Muszyna years rather than three years before them. The document is followed here; her account is kept beside it.
- **1937-06-22** — Shimon (Szymon) Rapaport born in Lwów: His own post-war survivor card gives 22 June 1937 in Lwów. Family memory had placed his birth in "Mosina" — the town that turned out to be Muszyna — but the card is explicit, and it predates the memoir by forty years.
- **1938-01-01** — David Rapaport in Lwów (1 Jan 1938): Per the 1946 Brussels DP card, David's place of residence on 1 January 1938 was Lwów, Poland.
- **1939** — Leah in Przemyśl, working under Soviet administration: After war broke out, Leah fled to Przemyśl. The city was DIVIDED across the San River — German on one side, Soviet on the other. Leah worked on the Soviet side as a teller and in the passport section. Visited David's parents (Berisz and Rebeka) there.
- **1939-09-01** — World War II begins — Germany invades Poland: Germany invades Poland on 1 Sep 1939. USSR invades from the east on 17 Sep 1939, per the Ribbentrop-Molotov Pact. Eastern Poland (including Lwów, Bolechów, Nadwórna) falls under Soviet occupation.
- **1941** — David escapes the Nadwórna ghetto: David had a work permit to a Nadwórna sawmill (likely the 'essential worker' tag mentioned in family memory). He escaped in a railway wagon — hidden in a niche he carved out between wooden logs — and reached Lwów.
- **1941** — David and Leah in Lwów under false identities: After the Germans took Lwów in 1941, David and Leah survived under fake identities. Leah lived at Legionów Street 24 under 'Hormek' false documents — also used the name 'Maria Cizlik' (a borrowed identity). She managed an art/antiques store. David worked in railway / road construction. They met on 'Rapaport Street' in Lwów. They lived apart for periods. David's sister Lota was also in Lwów.
- **1942** — David stripped of Polish citizenship: DP card notes 'deforce' (deprived) in 1942 — and Kasia (the French expert) read it as 'deported in 1942'. Likely indicates either deportation or formal denaturalization.
- **1946-04-09** — David registered as Displaced Person in Brussels: David Rapaport registered on the DIPIS (Displaced Persons) file at 'B.' (Brussels/Belgium) on 9 April 1946. Listed as married, Polish, Jewish, forestry engineer. Desired destination: Palestine or USA. He signed the card himself. Later stamped by the Belgian Repatriation Commission of the Ministry of Public Health and Family in 1969.
- **1946-08-28** — Dov Bernard Rapaport born in Brussels: Son of David and Leah, brother to Shimon. Born after the war ended.
- **1947-04-02** — David and Shimon sail from Sète on the Theodor Herzl: The ex-British cable-layer HMS Guardian, built of metal in Newcastle in 1907 — 1,768 tons, coal-fired, eight knots flat out — carrying 2,641 ma’apilim gathered from camps in France and Belgium. Her commander was Mecca Limon; the radio operator, the "Gideoni", was Nachman (Bob) Burstein; and a party of journalists sailed with her under Moish Perlman, later head of the Israeli government press office. Lusia stayed behind in Brussels with the eight-month-old Dov.
- **1947-04-13** — The Theodor Herzl is boarded by the Royal Navy: Intercepted off the coast by HMS Haydon and HMS St Brides Bay. The Palyam account calls it one of the most severe battles of the whole Aliyah Bet operation. Three ma’apilim were killed by gunfire and their names are known: AHARON DOV, PINCHAS WEISS and MENACHEM SAMET. The British commanders’ own report reads: "The most unusual feature of the arrest was the level of employment of firearms by the boarding party: of over a score of casualties among the immigrants, ten suffered gunshot wounds, of which two died at the time and one died later." The wounded and the sick were taken to the Atlit detention camp; everyone else was deported to Cyprus. David and nine-year-old Shimon were aboard.
- **1947-08-22** — Shimon released from the Atlit detention camp: The release certificate is printed in his mother’s memoir on page 64 and is dated at Haifa, 22 August 1947: "This is to certify that RAPAPORT SH… whose photograph is hereby attached, has been released from ATHLIT Camp… on authority of the Palestine Government. He/she will be registered as an immigrant to Palestine in due course." He was ten years old. This archive recorded the document as missing and the year as 1948 until August 2026, when the memoir plates were read.
- **1947—1948** — David and Shimon interned at Karaolos, Cyprus: The camps north of Famagusta held 53,510 Jews behind wire between 1946 and 1949, and some two thousand children were born inside them. David and Shimon were there about eight months.
- **1948** — Lusia and Dov sail for Israel on the Kedma: She had stayed in Brussels more than a year with the baby, working, while David and Shimon were behind wire in Cyprus. The Haifa burial register records her year of immigration as 1948 and David’s as 1946 — the two-year gap the memoir describes. Dov’s first journey was with his mother, alone. The memoir names the ship — קדמה, the Kedma — but not the port she sailed from, and gives no date beyond the year. The memoir names the ship — קדמה, the Kedma — but not the port she sailed from, and gives no date beyond the year.
- **1948** — The family is together again in Haifa: Lusia and Dov reach Haifa and are reunited with her parents, who had left for Palestine fifteen years earlier and whom she had not seen since. They went from the ship straight to her parents’ small flat on Shalom Street; the family later lived on Abbas Street, and later still on Moriah Street.
- **1948-05-14** — The State of Israel is declared: The Cyprus detainees were released to Israel over the months that followed.
- **1950s** — The apartment on Moriah Street, bought with German reparations: David worked as a customs broker in Haifa — the trade his son Dov, and later his grandson Doron, would follow.
- **1958** — David and Lusia travel to West Berlin over the reparations: The memoir records David wrestling with it: part of him could not forgive the Germans, and part of him argued it would be wrong to refuse what was lawfully owed. The memoir does not date the trip; the late 1950s is our estimate.
- **1967-04-17** — Shimon marries Tamar (Tami) Bugin in Tel Aviv: Shimon — the boy who spent two and a half years in a wooden crate in Lwów — became a journalist, working for Ma’ariv from the Tiberias and Haifa bureaus.
- **1975-05-16** — Dov marries Dalia in Haifa: Read from the ketubah itself on 2 August 2026: "here, in the holy community of Haifa, may it be rebuilt speedily in our days" — the place is written into the document. The year is 5735; the groom is Dov son of Menachem David, the bride Dalia daughter of Dov Yosef. A machine reading in June 2026 had given Brussels and flagged an oddity, that the document said "on Shabbat" while the date fell on a Friday. Both were errors of reading rather than of the scribe: the printed formula is "on the [Nth] day of the week", and filled in for Friday it reads exactly as it should. The compiled family tree’s 16 January 1974 in Haifa had the town right and the date wrong.
- **1986** — Lusia tells her story — the memoir is recorded: It began with a school assignment: her granddaughter Hadas approached her teacher, Esther Weiss, who then interviewed Lusia. Ninety pages in Hebrew. Without them almost nothing in this archive between 1939 and 1948 would have a voice.
- **1990-08-29** — David (Memek) Rapaport dies in Haifa: 8 Elul 5750. Buried at Sde Yehoshua cemetery, section ו, row 22, grave 102ד. He was 78. His death notice ran in Ma’ariv the following day.
- **1996-12-28** — Leah (Lusia) Rapaport dies in Haifa: 18 Tevet 5757. Buried beside David at Sde Yehoshua, grave 102ג — the two graves adjacent. Her burial record names her father Eliyahu and her mother Matilda, the same parents her memoir opens with. She was 83.
- **2026-05-04** — Family Tree research begins: Magda connects Dalia, Dana, Doron and Daniel with Basia (Polish genealogist) over WhatsApp. The 'Family tree' group is created. The goal: trace David, Leah, and Shimon Rapaport's journey for Dov's 80th birthday in August.
- **2026-05-08** — Brussels DP card identified: Basia finds a DIPIS Displaced Persons card for David Rapaport, born 25.12.1911, registered in B. on 9.04.1946, headed for Palestine or USA. The date of birth matches exactly. The card lists Cieszyn/Tesin as birthplace and parents Bernard and Regina née Grunfeld — which conflicts with family information.
- **2026-05-15** — BREAKTHROUGH: David's birth record found in Nadwórna: Ms. Kasia at the Jewish Historical Institute (ŻIH) in Warsaw finds David's birth certificate in Nadwórna. Confirms: parents Berisz Rapaport and Rebeka Griffel, daughter of Leizor Griffel and Sara née Chajes — merchants of Nadwórna. ALSO finds Rebeka's own birth certificate (1888 Nadwórna). The 'Cieszyn' on the DP card was a transcription error. Two new generations and the verified birthplace — all on one day.
- **2026-05-21** — 🗺️ Every Virtual Trip card now shows the address in Google Street View: All 13 Virtual Trip cards now embed two side-by-side iframes: the OpenStreetMap location plus a Google Street View pegman view at the exact coordinates — labelled 'How it looks today'. No API key required. Click any card to see the place AS IT LOOKS RIGHT NOW.
- **2026-05-21** — 👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 Attribution updated — Dalia, Dana, Doron and Daniel as equal partners: All attribution language throughout the archive (events, people, places, documents, hypotheses, research center, multilingual UI strings) has been updated to credit Dalia (mother), Dana, Doron and Daniel Rapaport as equal partners in this birthday gift to Dov. Mother and three children — one family voice.
- **2026-05-21** — 🏕️ Cyprus camp numbers DOCUMENTED — Karaolos 55/60/61/62/63 (summer tents) vs Dekhelia (winter huts): British internment camps in Cyprus were organised in two clusters: SUMMER tent camps numbered 55, 60, 61, 62, 63 at Caraolos/Karaolos north of Famagusta, and WINTER Nissen-hut camps at Dekhelia/Decauville near Larnaca (~50 km away). 53,510 Jews detained 1946-1949; ~2,200 children born in captivity. David's family was held ~8 months. JDC Archives only holds the Aug 1948 - Feb 1949 birth lists — for the family's roster look at Yad Vashem and USHMM databases.
- **2026-05-21** — 🚢 The Theodor Herzl ship VERIFIED — ex-HMS "Guardian", Newcastle 1907 (NOT Exodus 1947): The Aliyah Bet ship that carried David, Lusia, Shimon and infant Dov from Sète to Cyprus on 2 April 1947 was the ex-British cable-laying ship HMS/SS "Guardian" — built 1907 by Swan Hunter & Wigham Richardson in Newcastle, 1,768 tons, triple-expansion engine. Acquired by Mossad LeAliyah Bet in 1946 and refitted at Marseille. Distinct from the SS Exodus 1947 (ex-President Warfield), with which it is routinely conflated.
- **2026-05-21** — 🏠 Lusia's wartime apartment building IDENTIFIED — Prospekt Svobody 24, Lviv: ulica Legionów 24 in 1942-1944 Lwów is today's Prospekt Svobody 24, Lviv: a four-storey Biedermeier stone kamienica built 1836-1837 by architect Johann Salzmann for F. Adamski. Polish national poet Wincenty Pol lived there 1856-1866. Lusia rented an upper-floor apartment as 'Maria Cizlik' while a German military base fired guns from across the central promenade. Current photo of the actual building now embedded in the Virtual Trip card.
- **2026-05-23** — Berisz’s 1924 passport application names his parents: The paternal line had been a dead end even for Edward Gelles after twenty-five years. Basia found the passport file, and with it Moses Saul Rapaport and Menukha — and a photograph in which Dana saw a resemblance to her father.
- **2026-06-01** — Three Tarnów marriage deeds independently name the apical couple: Read from the scans by Claude vision: the 1919, 1920 and 1923 marriages of three of Berisz’s sisters, each naming "Moses and Menucha" as their parents.
- **2026-06-04** — A living cousin on the paternal side — and she wants to meet Dov: Yehudit Shlomit, daughter of Zwi Ayalon Nussbaum and named after Freida, Berisz’s sister. She worked at Yad Vashem and had been looking for our branch for years — she once tried to reach Dov through a school essay Dana wrote at twelve. Dana spoke with her for an hour.
- **2026-06-10** — Jente survived — the 1941 list of Jews in Liège: Her daughter Szewa appears on the 1941 census at 5 Place Delcour, and the 1946 Liège marriage record states that the bride’s mother, Jenta Rapaport, was living together with her daughter. A great-grand-aunt whose fate had been blank.
- **2026-07-07** — Szabse Rapaport, rabbi of Dąbrowa Tarnowska: Basia, from the Tarnów notarial records: "they convinced me that Szabse was your ancestor." He served until 20 April 1884 — and it explains at last why Berisz’s passport registers the family commune as Dąbrowa, a town nobody could account for. The same evening, from a different direction entirely, Dana produced a record naming Schabse with Figa/Yete.

## pl_kolomea

Birthplace of Sarah Matel Chajes (Eliezer Griffel's wife), connecting the family to the Chajes rabbinical dynasty of Kolomea.


## pl_vienna

Birthplace of cousin Edward Gelles (1927) — published genealogist of the Griffel-Nadwórna dynasty. Also where the Griffel timber business reached commercial Vienna.


## pl_new_york

Several Griffel-line cousins of David Memek Rapaport settled in NYC: Yehuda Nir (1930-2014, author of 'The Lost Childhood'); Dr Steven Lamm (b.1948, NYU Langone); Andrew Griffel (attorney, splits time with Israel).


## pl_london

Home of cousin Edward Gelles (b.1927), the published genealogist of our maternal Griffel-Nadwórna dynasty.


## pl_mainz

Original home of the Rapoport dynasty progenitor in the 15th century, before the family fled to Italy.


## pl_porto_italy

Where the Rapoport family takes its name. The dynasty's founder settled here after fleeing Mainz.


## pl_mallorca

Birthplace of the documented medieval Rapapa ancestors. The 1305 conspiracy to rescue a Jewish girl from forced conversion-by-marriage was led here by Dr Vidal Rapapa. Court physician Dr Jucef Salomon Rapapa served King Jaime III here. DNA testing confirmed living Mallorcan Chueta family ↔ US Rappoport family direct mitochondrial link.


## pl_portobuffole

Where R. Yechiel Michael ha-Kohen Rapa was born in 1502. His son Isaac 'HaMoel' was the first to use the surname 'Rapa-Porto' (~1550), creating the modern Rapaport name.


## pl_verona

Where R. Avraham Menachem ha-Kohen Rapa-Porto published Mincha Belulah in 1594, introducing the raven + priestly blessing family emblem still used by descendants today.


## pl_padua

Where the Katzenellenbogen rabbinical dynasty was founded. MaHaRaM Padua served as Chief Rabbi of Padua until 1565.


## pl_brest

Home of Saul Wahl Katzenellenbogen (the legendary 'King for one night' of Poland) and his rabbinical descendants.


## pl_tarnobrzeg

Where Chawa Wahl (1877-1941), David Mendel Griffel's wife, was born — into the Tarnobrzeg Wahl line documented as descending from Saul Wahl Katzenellenbogen.


## pl_stanislawow

Birthplace of Dr Chanan Rapaport (1928-2022), the Rapaport family genealogist. Regional capital of the voivodeship containing Nadwórna. Two known Rapaport households here in the 1920s — Berisz at Kościuszki 4 (1924), and a Chaim Samuel Rapaport family from 1916 (cousin candidate).


## pl_radomysl_wielki

BIRTHPLACE of Berisz Rapaport (1886, the family patriarch's son who married Rebeka Griffel) and his siblings Alte Leja (1882), Jente (1887), Freida Amalia (1888) and the younger Rebeka (1896). Source: family-built descendants tree of Moses Saul Rapaport + 1924 passport application (USHMM RG-31.064M) which gives Berisz's birthplace as Radomyśl Wielki. Notable for the unusually high proportion of Jewish residents — Radomyśl produced multiple famous figures including: Eliezer Margoshes (writer/historian, descendant of Solomon Luria); Reuven Iceland (Yiddish/Hebrew poet); Samuel Margoshes (Zionist Organization of America chairman); also reportedly the paternal ancestors of Arthur Miller and grandparents of Steven Spielberg.


## pl_auschwitz

Where Berisz / Bernard Dov Rapaport (paternal great-grandfather) was murdered. Confirmed by Auschwitz Museum victim database record #188161, transport #689. URL: https://victims.auschwitz.org/victims/188161. His sister Alte Leja Rapaport (m. Turkel) was also murdered at Auschwitz 1942.


## pl_olchowce

Site of the 1908 religious marriage of Berisz Rapaport × Rebeka Griffel per Edward Gelles's Griffel pedigree. The civil marriage was registered on 21 December 1911 in Nadwórna; the religious wedding (kiddushin) at Olchowce three years earlier is consistent with Jewish-Galician practice where ritual and civil marriages were sometimes separated by years.


## pl_tarnow

Source-of-origin city for the paternal Rapaport line — newly identified 23 May 2026 by Basia via the 1924 passport application. Tarnów was an established Jewish center within the Austro-Hungarian Kingdom of Galicia. Folwarczna 9 → Waryńskiego St. name-change is a standard 20th-century Polish street rename. The earlier Round-2 deep-research hypothesis that Berisz was born in eastern Galicia (Pasieczna/Loyowa/Pniow/Strymba/Bukowinka) is REFUTED.


## pl_dabrowa

Suggests the Rapaport line may extend further back into Dąbrowa Tarnowska, ~30 km north of Tarnów city. Worth searching Dąbrowa Tarnowska Jewish records for earlier Rapaports / Schiffs.


## pl_kosov

Founding seat of the Hager Kosov-Vizhnitz Hasidic dynasty. Birthplace of Reb Chaim Hager of Ottynia (1863-1931).


## pl_ottynia

Where Reb Chaim Hager (1863-1931) was rebbe. Eliezer Griffel and Berisz Rapaport prayed at the Ottynia kloiz.


## pl_vorokhta

Birthplace of Mollie Mehrbaum, an in-law of the Nadwórna Griffel family.


## pl_skole

Birthplace of Shlomo Goldfischer (Dalia's father; Dana, Doron and Daniel's maternal grandfather, b.23 November 1909). Skole is in the same Galician Carpathian region as Bolechów, Stryj, Dolina + Nadwórna — connecting Dana, Doron and Daniel's paternal (Rapaport-Weitzner) and maternal (Goldfischer) lines to the same small Jewish world of eastern Galicia.

Home of the Horowitz family who married into our paternal line (Mendel × Jente Rapaport, 1920).

Home of the Zylberfenig family. Sane Zylberfenig (b. Nov 1888 Płońsk, son of Abram + Pesi) married Berisz's sister Rebeka in Tarnów 1923.

# Every picture, with its address

Download any of these directly. **Never put a name on anything marked UNIDENTIFIED.**

## The memoir plates (15) — printed inside Lusia’s own book

- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_lajka_on_horse.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): ״לייקה״ על הסוס  
  Translation: “Lajka” on the horse  
  Note: The earliest photograph of Lusia the archive holds, and the only one from before the war. Two paragraphs above it the book explains the name: her father called her wild, “like a naughty boy”, but Lajka was what she was called — and she writes that she never knew what it meant. Bolechów.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_david_wedding_1938.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): מזל טוב! לוסיה וממק ביום חתונתם  
  Translation: Mazal tov! Lusia and Memek on their wedding day  
  Note: One of only two photographs of David known to exist. The memoir places the wedding in February 1938 on a day of ceaseless snow — it calls it a “white wedding” because the snow covered everything in pure white. The civil marriage record says 10 February 1935 in Bolechów. The month agrees; the year and the town do not. The archive records the conflict and does not settle it.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_baby.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): שמעון התינוק  
  Translation: Shimon the baby  
  Note: Shimon was born in 1937. The chapter this sits in is called “The first signs of antisemitism”, and it opens with Lusia wheeling him in his pram beside the river at Muszyna.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_family_dog.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): שמעון ליד הכלב המשפחתי  
  Translation: Shimon beside the family dog  
  Note: The lines directly above this photograph record what became of the dog: one day it bit the Germans’ cook, and was shot dead on the spot.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_holding_baby_dov.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): שמעון ״הגדול״ מחזיק בידיו את אחיו התינוק, דב  
  Translation: Shimon “the big one” holding his baby brother, Dov, in his arms  
  Note: Brussels. The memoir puts Dov’s birth in 1947; the records give 28 August 1946, and the records are right — so Shimon was nine here, not eight. Two and a half years of his own childhood had been spent hidden in a crate.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_brussels_school.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): שמעון על ספסל בית-הספר בבריסל  
  Translation: Shimon on the school bench in Brussels  
  Note: Brussels, about 1946-47. He was nine or ten. Two and a half years of his childhood had been spent hidden in a crate, and here he is at a school desk.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_atlit_release_1947.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): תעודת השחרור של שמעון ממחנה המעצר בעתלית  
  Translation: Shimon’s release certificate from the Atlit detention camp  
  Note: A dated document and a photograph of him at once. Haifa, 22 August 1947, signed by the Director of the Immigration Office: “This is to certify that RAPAPORT SH… whose photograph is hereby attached, has been released from ATHLIT Camp… on authority of the Palestine Government. He/she will be registered as an immigrant to Palestine in due course.” He was ten years old, and had been interned first at Karaolos on Cyprus and then at Atlit.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_dov_cousin_akiva.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): הפגישה, הפגישה שכזאת: האחים שמעון (משמאל) ודוביק (מימין) עם בן־דודם עקיבא אלדר  
  Translation: The reunion, what a reunion: the brothers Shimon (left) and Dovik (right) with their cousin Akiva Eldar  
  Note: The brothers meeting again. David and Shimon had gone ahead and been interned; Lusia and Dov followed on the ship Kedma, landing at Haifa. The caption names Akiva as their cousin. The archive records an Akiva born to Lusia’s sister Feige and Israel Englard, which would make him a first cousin — but that the surname Eldar belongs to the same man is not established.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_with_her_father.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): לוסיה עם אביה בישראל  
  Translation: Lusia with her father, in Israel  
  Note: Elias Weitzner had emigrated in the 1930s while she stayed behind to finish her studies; they had not seen each other since. The page beside this photograph records her walking down a Haifa street, seeing a policeman in uniform, and going rigid — until her father calmed her: “Here everyone is Jewish. The policemen are Jewish too.”
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/matel_weinreb_portrait.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): אמא של לוסיה  
  Translation: Lusia’s mother  
  Note: Matel Weinreb of Dolina, daughter of the innkeeper Samuel Weinreb. She married Elias Weitzner at Dolina in 1905. This is the only photograph of her the archive holds.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_in_israel.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): לוסיה נראית נינוחה ומאושרת במדינת ישראל  
  Translation: Lusia looks relaxed and happy in the State of Israel  
  Note: The chapter is about the one-room flat on Abbas Street in Haifa — a small kitchen, the toilet outside, 250 steps to climb with the shopping, and no bus that came that way. She writes that she was happy in it.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_purim_costume.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): לוסיה כ״נערה מהוואי״ בנשף פורים שערך בביתה  
  Translation: Lusia as a “Hawaiian girl” at the Purim party held at her home  
  Note: The flat at Moriah Street 93 in Haifa, bought with the German reparations money. They cleared the furniture into the garden; she made the costume herself out of raffia; Memek handled the drinks and the music, and they danced until the small hours.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/rapaport_family_picnic.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): משפחת רפפורט בפיקניק. מימין לשמאל: ממק, שמעון, לוסיה ודוביק  
  Translation: The Rapaport family on a picnic. Right to left: Memek, Shimon, Lusia and Dovik  
  Note: The only photograph in this archive that holds all four of them at once — David, Lusia, Shimon and Dov, together and out of danger. Israel, undated; the surrounding chapter covers Lusia’s eleven years at the post office.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_bnai_brith_delegate_card.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): כרטיס הציר של לוסיה לועידת ״בני־ברית״  
  Translation: Lusia’s delegate card for the B’nai B’rith convention  
  Note: A “delegate certificate” issued by B’nai B’rith in Israel, Galilee Lodge, Jerusalem, naming her as delegate of the Hermon lodge to the forty-fifth annual convention. She ran the lodge’s activity for children aged 6 to 13 — sports equipment for a school on the Carmel, a collective bar mitzvah, and a set at her own home for the neighbourhood.
- https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_back_cover_portrait.jpg  
  Caption as printed (Hebrew): לוסיה רפפורט (בתמונה)  
  Translation: Lusia Rapaport (pictured)  
  Note: The back cover of the book. It carries no caption of its own — the blurb beside it names her, “Lusia Rapaport (pictured)”. Undated; the memoir was dictated in 1986. It is the clearest photograph of her the archive holds.

## Portraits of named people (12)

- Dov Rapaport — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_holding_baby_dov.jpg  
  Dov as an infant in Brussels, held by his brother Shimon — from their mother’s memoir, page 61, captioned “Shimon ‘the big one’ holding his baby brother, Dov”. He was born 28 August 1946.
- David (Memek) Rapaport — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_david_wedding_1938.jpg  
  David (Memek) with Lusia on their wedding day — from her memoir, page 15, captioned “Mazal tov! Lusia and Memek on their wedding day”. One of only two photographs of him known to exist. The memoir dates the wedding February 1938 in Muszyna; the civil record says 10 February 1935 in Bolechów. Unresolved.
- Leah Lusha Rapaport (née Weitzner) — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_lajka_on_horse.jpg  
  Lusia on horseback in Bolechów, before the war — from her own memoir, where the caption reads “Lajka on the horse”. Lajka was her nickname; the same page says she never knew what it meant. Her father called her wild, “like a naughty boy”. This is the earliest photograph of her the archive holds.
- Shimon Rapaport — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/shimon_brussels_school.jpg  
  Shimon at his school desk in Brussels, about 1946-47 — from his mother’s memoir, where the caption reads “Shimon on the school bench in Brussels”. He was nine or ten. Two and a half years of his childhood had been spent hidden in a crate.
- Berisz / Bernard / Dov Rapaport — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/berish_rapaport_portrait_1924.jpg  
  Berish Rapaport, 1924 — the photograph pasted onto his own qualification certificate that July, when David was twelve.
- Alte Leja Rapaport (m. Turkel) — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/alte_lea_rapaport_turkel_portrait.jpg  
  Alte Lea Rapaport, later Turkel — David’s aunt, one of Moses Saul’s daughters. A studio portrait, undated; the dress and the hat put it near her 1909 marriage to Jechiel Turkel.
- Freida Amalia Rapaport — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/freida_amalia_rapaport_portrait.jpg  
  Freida Amalia Rapaport, later Nussbaum — David’s aunt. The only likeness of her the family holds, and it survives as a thumbnail; it is published at the size it survives at rather than enlarged into something it is not.
- Zwi (Hersh Heschel) Ayalon Nussbaum — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/period/zwi_nussbaum_1945.jpg  
  Zwi (Hirsch Herman) Nussbaum, c.1945 — from his citizenship application
- Elias (Eliyahu) Weitzner — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/lusia_with_her_father.jpg  
  Elias Weitzner with his daughter Lusia in Israel — from her memoir, page 66, captioned “Lusia with her father, in Israel”. He had emigrated in the 1930s; she stayed behind to finish her studies, and the war came.
- Matel (Mathilde) Weitzner (née Weinreb) — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/memoir/matel_weinreb_portrait.jpg  
  Matel Weinreb — from her daughter’s memoir, page 67, captioned simply “Lusia’s mother”. The only photograph of her the archive holds.
- Lea Rapaport (m. Segal / Fridman) — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/documents/basia_2026_08/lea_rapaport_segal_portrait.jpg  
  Lea Rapaport, later Segal and then Fridman — David’s first cousin, daughter of his uncle Simon. Born 10 November 1912, eleven months after David.
- Inge Nussbaum (née Rosenbusch) — https://rapaportfamily.github.io/assets/research_images/period/inge_nussbaum_1945.jpg  
  Inge Nussbaum née Rosenbusch, c.1945 — from her husband’s citizenship application

## Dov’s four journeys back (120 photographs)


### Warsaw — 14–20 April 2015 (36)

The Old Town, the Barbican, the Royal Castle, the rebuilt squares — and the churches and streets of a city that was flattened and put back stone by stone.

### Przemyśl — 19 April 2015 (40)

The town where Berish and Rebeka were living when the war came. The camp death books give Przemyśl as Berish’s residence, and Lusia’s memoir says the Germans killed one of David’s parents here. Most of these frames are taken through a car windscreen, driving its streets slowly, in the rain.

### Brussels — 21–23 June 2018 (13)

Where Dov was born on 28 August 1946, four months after his father registered as a displaced person in this city and wrote “Palestine or U.S.A.” in the box for where he wanted to go.
