The day before yesterday passers-by in ul. Sapieżyńska witnessed summary justice done by Jews on one of the Griffel brothers, against whom criminal proceedings are now going on in the local court. As is known, the firm Griffel committed, some years ago, frauds running to millions, to the harm both of the state and of wide masses of the Jewish population, recruited mostly from the poorest classes. When the Griffel brothers were returning from court at about 4.30, a crowd of Jewish people pulled one of the brothers from a cab and beat him severely, thereby administering justice to themselves and summary punishment to the accused. It must be added that the Griffel case has been dragged through the court for five years and is still not finished. [The rest of this notice, and the article on the next page, argue from the case to conclusions about "Jewish ethics". The archive prints the record and does not adopt its voice. What the passage actually documents is that Jewish creditors were ruined, that they were angry enough to beat a man in the street, and that a hostile paper was glad of it.]
*criminal trial · strike · antisemitism in the reporting*

**16 July 1935 — Kurier Powszechny, year VIII, Kraków-Lwów, Tuesday 16 July 1935, No. 194**  
STANISŁAWÓW CHRONICLE. THE EPILOGUE OF A GREAT FRAUD. On 8 July the trial began before the criminal section of the local district court against members of the family of the late EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL — the epilogue of the million-scale fraud and bankruptcy of the firm Griffel in 1930. Despite the passing of five years the case has not ceased to interest local society, especially Jewish society, not only because of the great number of those harmed but because of the five-year composition period, which led to nothing, since those harmed have to this day not received even part of their claims. The Griffels were owners of refineries at Stanisławów and Skawina and of oil terrains in the Nadwórna district, co-owners of the "Babet" mine at Dźwiniacz and the "Wierbiąż" refinery at Kołomyja. Despite such great property the firm had worked at a loss since 1926, which did not prevent the owners from expanding and even engaging foreign capital. It came to the point where the Griffels, commanding great foreign capital, sought to create a good impression of the activity of their balance sheet and so to inspire confidence among the local population. The plan succeeded completely. As the appearance of prosperity was magnified, so grew the trust of the wide Jewish masses, who began to place their savings with the firm. To what degree the Griffels were able to bemuse their co-religionists is shown by the fact that those placing savings sought no guardianship security in banks or mortgages: an ordinary scrap of paper from the firm sufficed. As trust grew, so did the firm's obligations, and it had to turn money obtained from others to satisfying and paying interest on debts already contracted. No wonder that not only the Jewish poor but the intelligentsia, and various banks and institutions, fell for the "safe and good" investment. It is characteristic that faith in Griffel's business was supported and upheld by the late miracle-working rabbi of Ottynia, Heger, who enjoyed great authority among wide masses of his co-religionists. That the Griffels knew the real state of their affairs is shown by their commercial books, kept in such a way that outsiders could hardly make out the state of their finances. And although the bomb had finally to burst, the Griffels went on accepting deposits until July 1930. In that month they filed for composition proceedings, not forgetting a few days earlier to remove from Stanisławów, quietly and mostly by night, all their valuables and precious objects from their household furnishings. The Griffels also moved from Stanisławów to Kraków, where they declared insolvency before the court there, seeking thus to disappear from the eyes of the harmed Jewish population and to remove them, by the great distance, from influence over the proceedings. The efforts of Stanisławów advocates to have the estate transferred to Stanisławów were of no avail. The size of the fraud is shown by the figures: in the composition proceedings the passive state was fixed at about 14 million złoty, of which 9 million fell on Stanisławów and district. In the course of the proceedings the Griffels' property was valued at 5 million złoty and the debts at 13 million, so the deficit proved greater still. In the dock sat SZULIM GRIFFEL, OZJASZ GRIFFEL, Weisblum (the Griffels' general bookkeeping fixer), Blaustein, and Horowitz, the last a specialist in manufacturing promissory notes. The tribunal: vice-president Dr Cysarz presiding, with judges Słowik and Malinowski; prosecution by the head of the prosecutor's office, Goertz; defence by advocates Landau and Gestler of Lwów, Zasławski and Darm. The civil claim on behalf of some of those harmed is brought by advocate Jonas. A series of witnesses passed before the tribunal whose evidence was simply crushing for the accused — whether it was an old woman worn out by life who had placed her savings with the Griffels in the hope of material advantage, or representatives of the banks and institutions harmed: always the subject was the Griffels' clever fraudulent machinations and those of their pack of company clerks. The public that gathered in the great hall of the district court filled it to the brim with Jewish people recruited from the poor who had placed their savings with the Griffels. After the evidence, the prosecutor and the defence made a series of applications, and the tribunal, granting most of them, adjourned the trial indefinitely. And so, five years after the millions-scale frauds came to light, the moment of justice has again been put off. [This paper draws from the case explicit conclusions about "the ethics of that nation from which the accused come". Those passages are recorded here as what they are — the antisemitic framing in which much of the reporting reached its readers — and are not reproduced as commentary the archive shares.]
*criminal trial · antisemitism in the reporting · the rabbi of Ottynia*

**14 August 1935 — Chwila, evening edition, year 2, no. 294 (14 August 1935)**  
A NEW GRIFFEL AFFAIR. From Stanisławów it is reported: the echoes of the sensational Griffel trial held last month, adjourned for the examination of several dozen witnesses, have not yet died away, and already a new sensation must be recorded. Our reporter learns that a criminal information has been lodged with the local prosecutor's office against SZULIM GRIFFEL and associates. Those interested and harmed, whose number already reaches 100 persons, charge the Griffels and associates with doing unclean business with the estate's money. It appears that after the sale of the Korneuburg refinery the Griffels' intermediary and trustee J. Rosenheck transferred 100,000 zł to the account of the Municipal Savings Bank in Stanisławów, and that sum was to be divided among the small creditors in Stanisławów. Instead, however, of paying the small Stanisławów creditors sums on account of the 50 per cent already agreed, they were paid barely 15 to 20 per cent, with the remark that if they did not accept this transaction they would get nothing at all. The lodging of a new criminal information, charging the Griffels with sins not covered by the old indictment, has aroused understandable interest in Stanisławów.
*criminal trial · new charges*

**16 September 1935 — Dziennik Wileński, year 19, no. 255 (16 September 1935)**  
ONE MORE CLAIM AGAINST THE SWINDLERS GRIFFEL. The District Directorate of State Railways in Lwów has lodged a claim against the well-known swindlers and speculators GRIFFEL for the payment of 24 thousand złoty due on account of transport credits which the Griffels used in their time. So the series of the Griffels' million-scale frauds spreads ever wider, and the swindlers remain at liberty and unpunished.
*criminal trial · railway claim*

**20 November 1935 — Chwila, evening edition, year 2, no. 376 (20 November 1935)**  
PART OF THE INQUIRIES AGAINST THE GRIFFELS DISCONTINUED. Stanisławów, 20 November. We reported in Chwila the opening of new inquiries against the Griffels following a fresh information lodged with the Stanisławów prosecutor by one of the Griffels' creditors, Grzegorz Hausman. According to the information, the Griffels were said to have approached smaller creditors to buy out their claims for sums lower than those in the agreement concluded with them in the composition proceedings. We learn that the Stanisławów prosecutor's office has discontinued the inquiries conducted in this matter.
*criminal trial · inquiry closed*

**1935 — Advertisements section, page 127: a 1935 trade profile of the firm**  
ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL — STEAM SAWMILLS, TIMBER INDUSTRY, TIMBER EXPORT. Lwów, ul. Kopernika 5. THE ABOVE FIRM WAS FOUNDED IN 1923 and has transit warehouses at Gdynia and Gdańsk. The chief object of the undertaking is the export of coniferous timber in the form of sawn material (96.7 per cent of the whole export), in small quantity semi-finished goods (2.2 per cent) and logs. The firm shows its greatest expansion on the English market, which has a large capacity for timber and is well organised. The firm has a whole network of representatives on the English market and aims to introduce Polish timber there under a proper brand, which is steadily gaining in importance. Until now Polish timber went to the English market through Danzig exporters and intermediaries and was known as "DANZIG TIMBER". IN THE BUSINESS YEAR 1934/35 THE FIRM EXPORTED 60,000 TONNES OF MATERIAL TO ENGLAND, WORTH OVER 5 MILLION ZŁOTY. It also exported to other countries: while England accounted for 82.8 per cent, exports to other countries were — Holland 8.3 per cent, Germany 2.9, Switzerland 2.3, France 1.9, other countries 1.7. One of the firm's own producing establishments makes box sets both for domestic needs and for export. The owner of the firm is Mr Zygmunt Griffel, Lwów.
*timber export · the firm at its height*

**January 1936 — Chwila, evening edition, year 3, no. 413 (3 January 1936) and no. 435 (29 January 1936)**  
THE GRIFFEL TRIAL IN FEBRUARY. Stanisławów. The sensational trial of the former oil magnates GRIFFEL, interrupted last year, will begin in the first half of February before the tribunal of the Stanisławów court, presided over this time by the vice-president of the district court Kadów. The investigating judge's inquiries in Stanisławów have been completed; witnesses are now being examined in Vienna and Kraków. — Later: THE GRIFFEL TRIAL DEFINITELY IN FEBRUARY. The inhabitants of our town, among whom are a number of people harmed by the Griffels' bankruptcy, await with great interest the continuation of the trial interrupted last year. The continuation has now been definitely fixed for 10 February. The hearing will last four days. The tribunal will be presided over by vice-president W. Kadów. The defence is by Dr L. Landau of Lwów, Dr Zasławski and Dr Darm of Stanisławów.
*criminal trial*

**10 February 1936 — Chwila, evening edition, year III, no. 444 (8 February 1936) and no. 445 (10 February 1936)**  
BEFORE THE GRIFFEL TRIAL / THE STANISŁAWÓW OIL PANAMA BEFORE THE COURT. Stanisławów. The Griffels' defenders applied to the court to adjourn the trial, since on the 10th the Vienna court would begin examining witnesses in the Korneuburg matter. The Stanisławów court rejected the application, and on Monday the trial begins. In the dock, besides the brothers SZULIM AND OZJASZ, will sit Leizor Weisblum, the Griffels' financial adviser, Markus Blaustein and Leon Horowitz. — 10 February: the trial began today against SZALOM GRIFFEL, HERSZ GRIFFEL, Markus Blaustein and Leon Horowitz, heroes of the famous oil panama. It began although the letters rogatory for the Vienna witnesses had not yet arrived. As is known, A TRIAL AGAINST THE GRIFFELS IS TAKING PLACE TODAY AT THE SAME TIME IN THE VIENNA COURT. The examination of witnesses proceeded, directed at the detailed investigation of the frauds committed with the participation of the Mortgage Bank in Stanisławów: the accused Weissblum asked director Horowitz for an advance against a dozen or so tank-cars of cylinder oil destined for the Korneuburg refinery; the director agreed and ordered 7,000 dollars paid; the Korneuburg refinery did not redeem the oil and the goods returned to Skawina, where it turned out that it was not cylinder oil but gas oil. At the opening the presiding judge Kadów took the accused men's personal particulars, from which it appears that SZALOM GRIFFEL lately lived in Kraków and is a representative of the Kraków artificial-casing works "Seltra", and so earns the keep of his wife and child; his YOUNGER BROTHER HERSZ IS WITHOUT WORK AND IS KEPT BY HIS MOTHER IN KRAKÓW; the accused Weissblum, former financial adviser, now lives in Warsaw, is without work but is shortly to obtain a post with the firm "Iron" at Rymanów; the former cashier Blaustein has been without work since the Griffels' bankruptcy; and Leon Horowitz lives in Kraków, likewise without a post.
*criminal trial · what became of them*

**10 February 1936 — Chwila (10-11 February 1936, continued)**  
The indictment was read, taking almost an hour. The presiding judge then examined the first accused, SZALOM GRIFFEL, who does not admit guilt. At the end of 1924 and the beginning of 1925 he entered the undertaking as a trainee. He trained first in the refineries in Little Poland, then was transferred to Skawina; from Skawina he returned to Stanisławów, where he helped his father in business. HIS FATHER WAS INDEED ILL WITH PERNICIOUS ANAEMIA, but ran the business himself and settled the most important matters; only in some cases did the accused act for him. PRESIDING JUDGE: "And who replaced your father when he went abroad to the spas?" ACCUSED: "During his absence my elder brother MENDEL and I replaced him by turns. I only partly. In 1930 the firm broke down and bankruptcy followed. In July 1930 certain payment difficulties appeared. At that time the liabilities were 10 million złoty, of which we had nearly 7 million in cover. My father made certain efforts to settle with the creditors before the composition proceedings were opened, and even proposed a settlement at 100 per cent over five years. These negotiations, however, came to nothing." The presiding judge then examined the accused on the charges concerning him personally — frauds to the harm of the creditor Majer Haller, owner of a series of oil terrains at Sołotwina. Haller had proposed to the Griffels the purchase of terrains at Starunia for 5,000 dollars. Haller took 55 per cent of that business but gave no money, having no cash, and issued promissory notes for his share, which he constantly extended for want of cash. For the Griffels' convenience he also issued accommodation notes. As is known, it was Haller who brought about the criminal investigation against the Griffels. The head of the Stanisławów prosecutor's office, Goerz, then began his questions.
*criminal trial · the father was ill*

**11 February 1936 — Chwila, evening edition, year 3, no. 446 (11 February 1936)**  
THE STANISŁAWÓW GRIFFELS' PANAMA CONTINUED. Stanisławów, 11 February. On this second day of the trial the examination of witnesses began. The first to testify was Samuel Wein, a grain merchant and intermediary in promissory-note matters between the industrialist Lieberman and the Griffels. He testifies as to debts contracted with Mr Lieberman TO THE SUM OF 40,000 DOLLARS. The loan was to be paid in eight instalments, the last falling due in July 1930. At that time the accused HERSZ GRIFFEL came to the witness and asked for a delay, since a loan was hard to obtain, all the more as that loan had been contracted by the Griffels' Vienna branch. Next MAJER HALLER testified. PRESIDING JUDGE: "Do you know Chaim Griffel?" WITNESS: "Since 1922. In 1926 we bought oil fields at Starunia together. I had an option from the owner of those fields. I proposed the purchase to Griffel. I was assured 55 per cent of the shares in that transaction and was not to put money into the partnership; we were to divide the profit after the sale. Griffel put up the whole purchase price." PRESIDING JUDGE: "And what about the notes you issued? They came to almost 54,000 dollars." WITNESS: "Those were accommodation notes. THOSE NOTES DESTROYED ME. THROUGH THE GRIFFELS I BECAME A POOR MAN, I LOST MY WHOLE FORTUNE, AND IT IS KNOWN THAT I WAS ONCE A VERY RICH MAN. Old Griffel and the accused Ozjasz Griffel and Weissblum assured me that the old notes were being destroyed, and meanwhile they were put into circulation. I issued notes for 1,000 zł, and the Griffels stamped them at the tax office and turned 1,000-złoty notes into notes for 10,000 zł." Weissblum and the accused Ozjasz Griffel urged him to issue extension notes. "After the proceedings were declared I was in Kraków at the Griffels', where I received a list of my notes to a total of twenty-odd thousand dollars." Besides the oil-field business he also traded in crude with the Griffels; to this day he still owes the banks a great deal of money. After the break the presiding judge examined the former director of the Stanisławów branch of the Mortgage Bank, ADOLF HOROWITZ, concerning the frauds committed against that bank to the sum of 7,100 dollars: the bank advanced money against cylinder oil which, as later appeared, was not cylinder oil but gas oil, worth 80 per cent less. The trial continues.
*criminal trial · Haller ruined*

**11 February 1936 — Chwila, year 18, no. 6067 (11 February 1936)**  
THE STANISŁAWÓW PANAMA BEFORE THE COURT. WHAT YESTERDAY BROUGHT. The tribunal examined the accused SZALOM GRIFFEL. PRESIDING JUDGE: "Who drew up the balance sheets?" ACCUSED: "I do not know." ADVOCATE DR LANDAU: "Did you feel the firm collapsing some months before the bankruptcy?" ACCUSED: "It all came suddenly. Credit was refused us then." The accused went on to describe the transactions with the Mortgage Bank, the Municipal Communal Savings Bank and Haller. The court then examined the next accused, OZJASZ GRIFFEL, who does not admit guilt. HE DID NOT CONCERN HIMSELF WITH HIS FATHER'S BUSINESS AT ALL, BECAUSE HE DID NOT WANT TO BE A MERCHANT. HE ATTENDED THE GYMNASIUM IN BIAŁYSTOK, AND WHEN HIS FATHER FELL ILL HE WAS CALLED HOME TO HELP IN THE BUSINESS. After returning home he had some contact with the business but settled no matters on his own. When his father went abroad he received from him promissory notes for a large sum, issued in blank, for safekeeping. On hearing that Mrs Manheim intended to lodge an information, he went to her asking her to refrain. The accused LEIZER WEISSBLUM, the Griffels' confidant, also does not admit guilt; he had no knowledge of the Griffels' financial operations. PRESIDING JUDGE: "But you were the Griffels' right hand in concluding transactions. You gathered all the capital." ACCUSED: "Until 1923 I was an assistant bookkeeper." PRESIDING JUDGE: "Where did you learn bookkeeping?" ACCUSED: "At home." PRESIDING JUDGE: "Did you have any contact with the intermediaries?" ACCUSED: "I did, but I settled no matters with them myself, because I was only a messenger." PRESIDING JUDGE: "How much was your remuneration?" ACCUSED: "600 zł." PRESIDING JUDGE: "In that case you must have been a very able messenger." PROSECUTOR GOETZ: "You maintain you were a messenger and unfit to be a bookkeeper, but you were fit for negotiations with banks." ACCUSED: "Perhaps the prosecutor is surprised; what can I do, that is how I am." The next accused, MARKUS BLAUSTEIN, the Griffels' chief cashier, was one of the employees WHO HAD PLACED HIS OWN SAVINGS WITH THE GRIFFELS. That money was lost. Since the bankruptcy he has been without a post. He worked there as cashier for 10 years, making payments in and out, accepting deposits and keeping the record of individual notes in circulation. A curious detail from his evidence is that he also received instructions from Weissblum. The further accused LEON HOROWITZ denies being guilty in the matter of the Griffels putting into circulation the notes of his late father-in-law Rothblum.
*criminal trial · Ozjasz did not want to be a merchant*

**12 February 1936 — Chwila, year 18, no. 6068 (12 February 1936)**  
WITNESSES' EVIDENCE IN THE TRIAL AGAINST THE GRIFFELS. Stanisławów, 12 February. Yesterday afternoon the former director of the Stanisławów branch of the Mortgage Bank, ADOLF HOROWITZ, testified that in May 1930 Weisblum came to him and asked for an advance on 13 tank-cars of cylinder oil sent to the Korneuburg refinery. The oil had been ordered by the firm Adelsberg, which was bound to take it over and send the money. A few days later he learned that the Korneuburg refinery did not acknowledge the goods and would not take them at all. Asked about it, the Griffels calmed him; but since the oil went on standing and costs mounted, he was uneasy and sought buyers. Analysis showed that it was not cylinder oil but gas oil of much lower value. A year after the transaction the goods were bought by a certain Englaender; the purchase price did not even cover the costs, and on that transaction the bank suffered a loss of 6,000 dollars, besides 2,000 dollars on notes coming into the bank. Asked by the presiding judge, director Horowitz explains that it was SZULIM GRIFFEL and the accused Weisblum who conferred with the bank; OLD CHAIM EISIG GRIFFEL SPENT MOST OF HIS TIME SITTING WITH RABBIS. PROSECUTOR: "What part did Weisblum play with the Griffels?" WITNESS: "From what I observed, the firm used him for almost all business. He was a good professional. I regarded the accused Szulim Griffel as the soul of the business, though he did not have unlimited powers." — WITNESS IZAK MENDEL SCHAUM lost 5,000 dollars in business with the Griffels. He gave them a good cheque to realise and they gave him in exchange worthless notes which were never redeemed. That cheque was taken from Schaum by the accused HERSCH GRIFFEL a few weeks before the bankruptcy. — WITNESS REGINA FEIL handed the Griffels 800 dollars and received Rothblum's notes. This was six years after Rothblum's death. On handing over the notes the Griffels assured her that the firm Rothblum in Kraków was better still than their own. Rothblum was represented, as is known, by his son-in-law Leon Horowitz, who, asked by the presiding judge why he did not answer Miss Feil's letters, states that he did answer her. At which the witness declares in an agitated tone: "I state categorically that Mr Horowitz is lying impudently." Miss Feil testifies that she knows that when Rothblum died his assets were 1,100 zł and his liabilities 1,400 zł, and there was not even money for the funeral. — WITNESS STEFANJA MANHEIM lent the Griffels 2,500 dollars. When the crash came she received forged Rothblum notes. On learning this she wanted to lodge an information against the Griffels, AND THEN SZULIM GRIFFEL CAME TO HER, LAID A REVOLVER ON THE TABLE AND GAVE HER TO UNDERSTAND THAT HE WOULD KILL HIMSELF IF SHE LODGED THE INFORMATION. Witnesses Pinkas Wagschal, Marja Wilczyńska, a clerk of the Savings Bank, and Dawid Feil repeat evidence already given at the previous hearing.
*criminal trial · the revolver on the table*

**12 February 1936 — Chwila, evening edition, year III, no. 447 (12 February 1936)**  
THE DEFENDERS JOIN THE PROSECUTOR'S APPLICATION. Stanisławów, 12 February. The third day of the trial abounded in the sensational evidence of ZIOBROWSKI, director of the Municipal Savings Bank. From his evidence, which made a great impression, it appears that in 1930 the accused Weissblum came to him and produced three promissory notes for 18,500 Swiss francs, asking for credit for the Griffels. Director Ziobrowski placed the savings bank's endorsement on them and sent them for rediscount to the Bank of Poland. The Bank of Poland, however, refused credit, BECAUSE THE NOTES BORE TRACES OF ERASURE. The notes were therefore returned and Ziobrowski informed Weissblum, handing them back with the savings bank's endorsement struck through in chemical pencil. The Griffels afterwards removed those crossings-out and put the notes into circulation, discounting them at the Bank Zachodni in Kraków. Director Ziobrowski testifies that the Savings Bank had great confidence in the Griffels and that he did not suppose so solid a firm would take the bank in in such a "swindling" way. On news of the bankruptcy he went at once to Kraków and began negotiating for the return of the money. As a result, IDA, WIFE OF THE LATE EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL, being co-owner of a tenement house in ul. Pieracki in Stanisławów, transferred that house, worth 4,000 dollars, into the Savings Bank's ownership. Altogether the bank lost 60,000 zł on business with the Griffels. The court then examined the chief intermediary who supplied the Griffels with money, JOEL EHRLICH, who had not appeared at the July hearing. At the outset prosecutor Goerz applied for this witness not to be sworn, as he is suspected of complicity in the Griffels' frauds, and noted that he would open a separate investigation against Ehrlich. The defenders Dr Landau and Dr Zasławski joined the prosecutor's application. The tribunal decided not to swear Ehrlich. Ehrlich stressed that he was an intermediary in lending money to the Griffels: HE SOUGHT OUT WEALTHY CITIZENS OF STANISŁAWÓW AND BROUGHT THE MONEY BORROWED FROM THEM TO THE GRIFFELS AT HIGH INTEREST, THE INSTRUCTIONS BEING GIVEN BY THE LATE EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL. He supplied the Griffels with that money for 12 years.
*criminal trial · the erased endorsement*

**13 February 1936 — Chwila, year 18, no. 6069 (13 February 1936)**  
WITNESSES TESTIFY IN THE GRIFFEL TRIAL. Stanisławów, 13 February. The witnesses Leib Ziper, an intermediary, and Jakób Hasa added nothing. The evidence of LEJBA WIESELMAN, the chief intermediary in buying oil terrains and the trusted man of the late Eisig Chaim Griffel, was interesting. In his evidence Wieselman states that IN 1923 THE GRIFFEL FIRM HAD NOT A PENNY OF DEBT AND THERE WERE THOUSANDS OF DOLLARS IN THE TILL. The whole misfortune of Eisig Chaim Griffel, the witness recalled, was caused by the building of the refinery at Korneuburg; that building broke the Griffels financially. The deceased tried to sell the Korneuburg refinery but the business did not come off. GRIFFEL FURTHER TOLD THE WITNESS THAT THE CAUSE OF THE GRIFFELS' FINANCIAL COLLAPSE WAS HIS ELDEST SON JAKÓB, WHO HELD THE POWER OF ATTORNEY. Asked by the presiding judge whether the accused HERSZ GRIFFEL meddled in the firm's affairs, the witness testifies that HE ATTENDED THE RABBINICAL SEMINARY IN BIAŁYSTOK AND INTENDED TO BECOME A RABBI; that he now sits in the dock he owes to the witness. Old Griffel, going abroad to a spa, asked the witness to replace him; the witness, fearing responsibility, proposed that Griffel bring his son Hersz from Białystok and that he would work with him. The witness categorically denies that Hersch Griffel forged promissory notes. Next DR MICHAŁ LAM, former industrialist and owner of the Lam banking house in Stanisławów, testified. He had known Eisig Chaim Griffel for over a decade. When in 1928 the banks refused the Griffels credit because he would not join the cartel, and his consumption and freight credits were struck out, Griffel turned to the Lams for help, which was given. THE STRUGGLE WITH THE OIL CARTELS ENDED IN GRIFFEL'S VICTORY. After some months Griffel signed an agreement with the cartels and ceased using the Lams' credit. In 1930 Griffel began working with Romania and again turned to the Lams, who went on financing his business. On the transactions with the Griffels the Lams lost 10,000 dollars, AND THAT WAS THE CAUSE OF THE COLLAPSE OF THE LAM BANKING HOUSE. The witness knows that after the bankruptcy the administrator Dr Woźniakowski received three and a half million to cover creditors; that money, however, was swallowed by costs and expenses.
*criminal trial · the father blamed Jakób · Ozjasz meant to be a rabbi*

**18 February 1936 — Warszawski Dziennik Narodowy, year 2, no. 48 (18 February 1936)**  
A GREAT FRAUDULENT PANAMA BEFORE THE COURT IN STANISŁAWÓW (from our own correspondent). [This is a nationalist paper and its framing is openly antisemitic throughout; it is recorded because it also carries facts, including the plainest surviving description of Eisig Chaim Griffel's standing in the community.] The hero of the fraudulent panama is the late EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL and his sons. Griffel was among the richest industrialists of our town: owner of refineries at Stanisławów, Skawina and Korneuburg near Vienna, owner of numerous oil terrains, FORMER TOWN COUNCILLOR OF STANISŁAWÓW, the most pious Jew in the town, the closest friend of the rabbi, HEAD OF THE LOCAL "AGUDA" ORGANISATION, well connected in oil-industry circles, a producer and processor of crude since 1921. Using the influence of the friendly rabbi and the Jewish masses' lack of confidence in Polish banking institutions, he accepted for interest and safekeeping the money of hundreds of Jewish families. Having organised a band of agents, he managed to borrow and take into safekeeping about 5 million złoty from the Jews of Stanisławów. By fraudulent manipulations he obtained credit of about 4 million złoty in all the banks of eastern Little Poland, and additionally took private creditors for a further 3 million. Particular emphasis should be laid on how Griffel used the credit of the Bank of Poland to finance export: learning that the Bank of Poland had a special credit quota for discounting foreign bills, he came to an understanding with the firm Silbergleit in Gliwice, which for a commission issued notes for 500 thousand złoty payable in Zurich, and Griffel discounted those notes at the Bank of Poland and other banks. All this went on until July 1930, when the firm filed for composition proceedings. Since part of the property, one of the refineries, lay at Skawina, the Griffels contrived that the Kraków court was recognised as competent, and proceedings were opened there — which freed the Griffels from the mass of harmed Jewish poor in Stanisławów. As composition administrator was appointed the advocate Dr Woźniakowski, married to a Jewess, and later the convert Dr Rychlewski. On the creditors' committee sat four Jewish advocates headed by the president of the Jewish community of Kraków. In the meantime Eisig Chaim Griffel died, and the inconsolable widow and children secured as guardian — for good remuneration — Colonel Dziekanowski. On numerous criminal informations from Stanisławów creditors, the Stanisławów court ordered the arrest of E. Griffel and both his sons at the moment the failure was declared. To general astonishment it turned out that Griffel had managed to obtain all the necessary documents and, through his representative, the former prosecutor Goertler in Lwów, to secure a safe conduct protecting him from arrest. Thus the arrested son of Griffel spent barely 24 hours in custody, since the Appeal Court in Lwów ordered his release. And since then, that is since 1930, six years, the swindlers remain at liberty, making use of the safe conduct of their father, who has since died. Last year the trial was held before the Stanisławów court but was interrupted to complete the investigation; it has only now been resumed. So the composition proceedings have lasted six years. The fees of the advocates, including Mr Dziekanowski, have grown to million-scale sums, while the creditors have so far received less than one per cent of their dues. […] The accused Szulim Griffel is now the representative of an artificial-casing factory in Kraków and continues to do good business. The hearing has now been adjourned for 14 days for want of the witnesses' evidence from Vienna; it is expected that the verdict will fall on the 27th of this month.
*criminal trial · antisemitism in the reporting · head of the local Agudath Israel*

**28 February 1936 — Chwila, year 18, no. 6084 (28 February 1936)**  
AN ACQUITTAL IN THE STANISŁAWÓW OIL PANAMA. Stanisławów, 28 February. Amid great interest the continuation of the Griffel trial began on Thursday. The interruption had been caused by the non-arrival of the records of the witnesses examined by the Vienna court, in particular the director of the Korneuburg refinery, Dr Zygfryd Adlersberg. At the opening of yesterday's hearing the evidence of the witnesses examined in Vienna was read: HERSZ KARMEL, Osiasz Haas, Juda Rinche, Anatol Rastenberg, engineer Edward Bruh, Maks Recht, Saul Kiesler and Ignacy Messinger. Their evidence brought nothing new. Since the Vienna court had been unable to examine Dr Adlersberg, who had left Vienna, prosecutor Goerz again applied for his examination and asked the presiding judge to interrupt or adjourn. Dr Landau for the defence opposed the application, maintaining that Adlersberg's evidence had been material only at the first hearing. The tribunal, after deliberation, rejected the prosecutor's application and continued. Prosecutor Goerz then spoke for an hour, illuminating the whole proceeding and demanding a conviction. The presiding judge then gave the floor to Dr Zasławski and Dr Landau. After the defenders' speeches the tribunal retired, and the verdict was announced by which THE ACCUSED SZALOM GRIFFEL, OZJASZ HERSZ GRIFFEL, LEISOR WEISSBLUM, MARKUS BLAUSTEIN AND LEON HOROWITZ WERE ACQUITTED OF THE CHARGE. The prosecutor gave notice of appeal.
*criminal trial · ACQUITTED*

**28 February 1936 — Chwila, year 3, no. 461 (28 February 1936)**  
THE GROUNDS OF THE GRIFFELS' ACQUITTAL. Stanisławów, 28 February. As we reported in the morning Chwila, the trial has ended which formed the judicial epilogue of the great oil panama of the firm "Eisig Griffel and Co." The case has dragged on for several years and was the subject of repeated hearings before the criminal tribunal, independently of the extremely tangled bankruptcy proceedings pending in the Kraków courts. The trial aroused great interest because of the number of those harmed, particularly in the Stanisławów area, WHERE THOSE HARMED INCLUDE A GREAT NUMBER OF WIDOWS AND ORPHANS WHO PLACED THEIR SAVINGS, SCRAPED TOGETHER FOR A RAINY DAY, WITH THE ONCE WEALTHY OIL MAGNATE. The criminal hearing ended in an acquittal. The grounds of the verdict state among other things: "In view of the contradictory evidence of Majer Haller, the court could not give credence to his testimony. As regards the Savings Bank, the acquittal of the accused Weissblum follows because Weissblum gave the note bearing the Savings Bank's endorsement to old Griffel and did not himself put it into circulation. As regards the Mortgage Bank, there were no grounds for finding an intention to defraud. In the matter of the notes forged to the harm of Mrs Manheim, the court acquitted Ozjasz Hersz Griffel, because it was not established who forged the notes, and the court did not credit Meier Haller's evidence. In the matter of the accused Horowitz, as regards the notes put into circulation by the firm Rothblum of Kraków, it is stated that the creditors themselves took no steps against Rothblum, so there is no proof whether that firm then had no property. Besides this, the course of the hearing and the evidence of some witnesses showed that the accused bear no guilt for the bankruptcy of the Griffel firm."
*criminal trial · ACQUITTED · the grounds*

**29 April 1936 — Chwila, evening edition, year 3, no. 512 (29 April 1936)**  
A NEW GRIFFEL AFFAIR. Stanisławów, 29 April. A new Griffel affair is on the scene. The news electrified those who lost their saved pennies. What is it actually about? After the bankruptcy the Griffels obtained a 50 per cent reduction in paying their obligations, and then, as a result of the fall of the dollar, they gained a further 30 per cent by that fall, so that very little remained to be paid in relation to the earlier sums. It has now appeared that among the Griffels' creditors there were touts who under terror forced creditors to accept trifling sums in satisfaction of the Griffels' obligations. The prosecutor's office has taken an interest, and on Tuesday the investigating judge examined SZULIM GRIFFEL, one of the heroes of the Griffel panama.
*bankruptcy · new inquiry*

**18 September 1936 — Przegląd Drzewny, weekly for timber-economy affairs, No. 11, Friday 18 September 1936, year I**  
The timber industry is represented in its own pavilion (Polish Timber Industry). We see there various stands of private firms, mainly of the district belonging to the Syndicate of Timber Interests in Lwów. Splendid specimens of pine, oak, ash and other balks were exhibited by the firms "Oikos" Ltd of Lwów, Szymon Ulam of Lwów, "Forestina" of Chodorów, Wohlmuth and Rapp of Stare Sioło. Sawn edged timber was represented by firms of great professional tradition such as J. Ph. Glesinger of Broszniów, Bracia Groedel of Skole, Polska Foresta S.A. of Nadwórna, and the Little Poland Timber Company of Lwów. WE FIND THERE THE STAND OF THE OUTSTANDING EXPORT FIRM ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL OF LWÓW. The further stage of timber working, in the form of parquet strips and beautifully finished floor patterns, was represented by the Krzemieniec Lyceum, the firm J. Thorn of Lwów, Jonasz Kuhmaerker of Lwów, and the Industrial Works of Count Stadnicki at Zawojowa. […]
*timber export · exhibition*

**31 October 1936 — Chwila, year 18, no. 6327 (31 October 1936)**  
FIRST TAX OFFICE IN STANISŁAWÓW, no. T.W. 1031/36. NOTICE OF AUCTION under § 83 of the regulation of the Council of Ministers of 25 June 1932. The First Tax Office in Stanisławów gives public notice that on 13 November 1936 at 10 a.m., on the railway siding of the refinery at ul. św. Michała 21 in Stanisławów, in order to settle the arrears due to the State Treasury from EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL, an auction sale will take place of 4 petroleum tank-cars, numbered 551,054, 552,917, 552,923 and 552,918. The estimated prices are: no. 551,054 — 3,000 zł; nos. 552,917 and 552,923 — 1,000 zł each; no. 552,918 — 500 zł. The seized tank-cars may be inspected on 12 and 13 November between 8 and 10 a.m. Head of the Tax Office, Karpów.
*tax · auction · the last of it sold*

**19 January 1937 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 4, Friday 22 January 1937, year II**  
DELIBERATIONS OF THE PRODUCERS OF BOX SETS. On the 19th of this month the ordinary annual general meeting of the Association of Producers of Box Sets was held in Warsaw under the chairmanship of the association's president Dr Witold Czerwiński, with numerous members taking part. After hearing the report on the association's activity, there was an extensive discussion on the export of box sets to foreign markets, IN PARTICULAR TO PALESTINE. The meeting expressed the conviction that, given the not very favourable arrangement of Polish-Palestinian trade, the presidium had obtained positive results, securing further development for the export of box sets to the Palestinian market. As regards export to the British market, the meeting resolved to take steps to make rational use of the interest recently aroused there by Polish box sets and to establish closer contact with British importers. The meeting then elected the audit commission, to which were elected Z. GRIFFEL, Sz. Schell and A. Geller, and approved the budget for 1937.
*timber export · exports to Palestine*

**9 July 1937 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 28, Friday 9 July 1937, year II**  
ADVERTISEMENT: "ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL — STEAM SAWMILLS, BOX FACTORIES, TIMBER EXPORT. LWÓW, ul. Kościuszki 7, tel. 278-56. Telegraphic address GRITIMB LWÓW. Production of planed and unplaned sawn spruce, fir and pine timber."
*timber export · advertisement*

**22 October 1937 — Monitor Polski, Official Gazette of the Republic of Poland, no. 244, Warsaw, Friday 22 October 1937, year XX**  
The firm "DENDROPOL", timber industry and trade, limited liability company in Lwów. Seat: Lwów, ul. Kościuszki 7. The objects of the undertaking are: a) to acquire and exploit forests and stands of timber; b) to conduct timber industry and trade in the widest sense of the word; c) to acquire immovables, and to acquire, found, lease and run sawmills and other establishments serving the timber industry and trade, by acquiring shares, holdings or otherwise; d) to conduct all kinds of industrial and commercial activities within the company's object and such as would further it; e) to found branches, agencies and representations in accordance with the relevant legal provisions. The share capital is 10,000 złoty. A partner may hold only one share. Elected members of the board: ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL, Jakub Bardach, Emil Selzer. The company is represented either by the board member Zygmunt Griffel alone, or by the two further board members only jointly.
*company register · Dendropol*

**December 1937 — Przegląd Polsko-Węgierski, organ of the Stefan Batory Polish-Hungarian Society, December 1937, year II, No. 5, Warsaw**  
POLISH EXPORTERS OF GOODS TO HUNGARY […] Fir boards […] TIMBER EXPORT ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL, LWÓW, Kopernika 5.
*timber export*

**1 January 1938 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 1/2, Saturday 1 January 1938, year III**  
EXPORT OF BOX SETS TO PALESTINE. The chief market for the products of our box-making industry has in recent years become PALESTINE, to which the export of box sets steadily grows. It is known, however, that Romania, the most considerable exporter of box sets to Palestine besides Poland, delivers goods more cheaply, enjoying the freight preference given by its favourable geographical position. If Poland has nevertheless managed to hold its position on the Palestinian market and even strengthen it, this is owed on the one hand to the strenuous efforts of our producers, and on the other to the circumstance that Poland, as a considerable buyer of citrus fruit, has naturally become Palestine's "natural" counterpart in the export of sets for packing that fruit. Our export grows steadily, and the transactions concluded in 1937 are estimated at over 3 million box sets. That quantity is to be supplied by the Little Poland firms: Bracia Groedel of Skole, Małopolski Przemysł Drzewny of Synowódzko Wyżne, J. Ph. Glesinger of Broszniów, Izydor Landau of Czarny Dunajec, ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL OF LWÓW (Segal, Zupnik and Wilf), Engel and Huttrer of Lesko-Łukawica and Lichtmann of Dobromil, and two Warsaw firms, "Las Kresowy" and the Union of Cooperatives "Społem". On 18 December the season of orange export from Palestine began and the first shipments are already arriving. Importers are already remitting payment for the fruit. It should be noted that in Palestinian box sets there is no internal competition: the requirements are still such that only some of the better-equipped box works can meet them, so only 9 firms take part in the export to Palestine, which makes it possible to "hold" the price.
*timber export · orange crates to Palestine*

**16 June 1938 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 25, Thursday 16 June 1938, year III**  
TRANSACTIONS. Rumours are going round in Lwów that the transaction between the State Forests and the firm "Silvinia" at Wygoda has been finalised. That firm, which owns enormous fir and spruce stands around Wygoda and a large sawmill, belongs to a group of foreign financiers and has now passed into the ownership of the State Forests for 8,000,000 złoty payable abroad; according to another version the price is 6,000,000 Swiss francs, likewise payable abroad, a condition the State Forests accepted. THE FIRM ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL IN LWÓW, WHICH BY CONTRACT UNTIL THE END OF 1939 HELD THE EXCLUSIVE REPRESENTATION OF THE FIRM "SILVINIA", ALLEGEDLY RECEIVED 300,000 zł IN COMPENSATION. The transaction was brokered by Mr Tomar of Lwów, permanently resident in Paris, the former director of that group of foreign financiers.
*timber · the state buys the forests*

**23 June 1938 — Posener Tageblatt, year 77, No. 140 (23 June 1938)**  
FROM THE POLISH TIMBER MARKET. Vilna. The market for sawn export timber remains quiet. Conclusions to England in particular are still rather sparse. RECENTLY THE FIRM GRIFFEL OF LEMBERG BOUGHT SEVERAL HUNDRED STANDARDS OF ENGLISH SPRUCE MATERIAL. Prices lay between 52 and 60 zł per unit franco Danzig, according to the dimensions of the goods. In general the circles of Vilna suppliers of sawn timber to England are of the opinion that business with England will revive in the summer.
*timber export*

**1938 — Polski Eksport-Import, monthly, year II, Warsaw, July-August 1938, No. 7/8, and "4000000", No. 6, September 1938, year I**  
POLISH EXPORTERS OF GOODS TO HUNGARY […] TIMBER EXPORT ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL, LWÓW, Kopernika 5. — And from the boycott sheet "4000000": "JEWS FROM LITTLE POLAND AS SUPPLIERS OF BOXES AND BOX SETS TO CHRISTIAN FIRMS. We learn that several considerable firms in Poznań cover their requirements for box sets and boxes in Jewish undertakings from Little Poland. It should be noted that in Poznań and Swarzędz there are factories which produce these box sets and boxes. The information gathered allows us to state that the little Jews Engel and GRIFFEL (from Little Poland) do considerable business in Poznań itself, reaching, as we have established, several tens of thousands of złoty a year. Our slogan is: NOT A PENNY TO A JEW, and we therefore call on all firms to cover their requirements for such packaging only in natively Polish box factories." [The paper's title is the number of Jews it wanted removed from Poland. It is kept here because it is part of the record of what the family was living through in 1938, and because it shows the business was still large enough to be worth boycotting.]
*timber export · boycott · antisemitism 1938*

**1 December 1938 — Krakowski Dziennik Wojewódzki, Kraków, 1 December 1938; and "4000000", No. 9, October 1938**  
OFFICIAL NOTICE: "GRIFFEL OZJASZ HENRYK, BORN 16 SEPTEMBER 1910 IN STANISŁAWÓW, of ul. Kołłątaja 2 — loss of the military book issued by the district recruitment command, Kraków city." [This fixes his birth date exactly, and confirms the tree against the wartime rescue list, which gave only "August 1910".] — And from the boycott sheet: "THE POWDER FACTORY 'PERSIL' IN BYDGOSZCZ BUYS BOXES FROM JEWS. The well-known firm Persil, producing the washing powders Henko and Persil, maintains commercial relations with the Jewish undertaking GRIFFEL OF LWÓW, and recently bought there boxes and box sets for about 300,000 złoty… The firm Persil, collecting money among Christian merchants, puts Polish złoty into Jewish pockets. For such conduct Persil must be duly boycotted… The Jewish firm Griffel of Lwów, which supplied the packaging, employs Jews in its factory and is based on exclusively Jewish capital."
*boycott · antisemitism 1938 · his birth date fixed*

**31 March 1939 — Biuletyn Ogólny, 1939, no. 90 (31 March), Polish Telegraph Agency PAT, Warsaw**  
Warsaw, PAT. All day today news has been flowing in from the whole country of the spontaneous resolutions of numerous organisations, social associations and industrial undertakings declaring sums for the purchase of THE ANTI-AIRCRAFT DEFENCE LOAN; private persons too are coming in crowds to the offices of the air-defence league, contributing according to their means. It is difficult, given the enormous list, to publish a register of all who have subscribed. The list below testifies clearly to the great sense of patriotism among the people of the whole of Poland. […] THE LWÓW INDUSTRIALIST ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL — 10,000 zł.
*the loan for the coming war · five months before the invasion*

**20 May 1939 — Dziennik Rozporządzeń of the commune of the royal free city of Stanisławów, year 4, no. 12 (20 May 1939)**  
List no. 3 received 2 seats, namely the following were elected. AS COUNCILLORS: 1) Dr Maksymilian Blumenfeld, aged 66, advocate, Sobieskiego 7; 2) Meier Leizer Scherzer, aged 65, merchant, Pierackiego 24. AS DEPUTY COUNCILLORS: 1) Izrael Izak Beegman, aged 46, property owner, ul. Krzywa 4; 2) DAWID MENDEL GRIFFEL, AGED 63, INDUSTRIALIST, PIERACKIEGO 24. [Aged 63 in May 1939 means born about 1876 — this is the elder Dawid Mendel Griffel, born 2 November 1875, brother of Rebeka Griffel who married Berish Rapaport; not his nephew of the same name born 1902. Four months before the war he was elected to the town council.]
*town council · four months before the war*

**23 June 1939 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 25/26, Friday 23 June 1939, year IV**  
Complaints multiply lately about worsening credit conditions in the retail timber trade. Pine sawn timber from Volhynia, for example, can today be obtained only for cash. The larger suppliers from eastern Little Poland have also tightened payment conditions: THE FIRM GRIFFEL SELLS GOODS ONLY ON RECEIPT OF 50 PER CENT OF THE PRICE IN CASH; Bracia Groedel of Skole act similarly. The firm Glesinger gives credit on 50-60 per cent of the price, setting conditions according to the buyer's solvency.
*timber · the last summer*

**29 July 1939 — Przegląd Drzewny, No. 32/33, Wednesday 2 August 1939, year IV**  
ROMANIAN TIMBER ON FOREIGN MARKETS. BUCHAREST, 29 July. Lately the larger production centres here, and Bucharest itself, are very often visited by representatives of English firms. These visits lead mostly to very considerable export transactions, and often even to the purchase of whole stocks of finished sawn timber at particular larger sawmills. An increasing number of sawmill owners are turning their production chiefly to the English market, especially those holding mountain spruce, since the present prices of £15 to £16 per standard of good spruce cif the English east coast, and payment in cash, are not only encouraging but simply unbeatable. […] BESIDES THE ENGLISH AND DANZIG FIRMS, THE FIRM ZYGMUNT GRIFFEL FROM POLAND ALSO WORKS ON A LARGER SCALE IN THE MARAMUREȘ REGION. […] Export of timber to Switzerland, Palestine, Greece, Turkey, Italy and Holland has not decreased, so local exporters suppose the total volume of timber export in the current campaign will rise by some 15-20 per cent over the last. […] Only the momentary lack of sufficient ships and the rising sea freight rates, especially towards England, tell unfavourably on the normal unwinding of export transactions. Local agents of the various shipping lines are of the opinion that by the end of August the situation in this respect will change for the better. [It was the last week of July 1939. It did not.]
*timber export · the last summer*


## 1940s

**21 November 1946 — Rzeszowski Dziennik Wojewódzki, year III, Rzeszów, 9 December 1946, No. 8**  
Item 48. ORDER OF THE CHAIRMAN OF THE MAIN COMMISSION FOR THE NATIONALISATION OF UNDERTAKINGS IN RZESZÓW of 21 November 1946, publishing the third list of undertakings passing into, or taken over as, the property of the State. […] 59. STEAM SAWMILL AND BOX FACTORY, ZARSZYN, district of Sanok — sawmill and box factory — GRIFFEL ZYGMUNT, RESIDENT IN LWÓW, OF POLISH NATIONALITY — art. 3.1.B. [The last document in this file. Sixteen years after his brother's firm collapsed, and seven years after the invasion, the communist state took what was left of Zygmunt Griffel's works. The register still lists him as resident in Lwów, which by then was no longer in Poland.]
*nationalisation · the end of the business · the last document*


## 2020s

**20-23 August 2026 — Basia's own note, page 120 of the press file: further Griffel material located in Polish state archives and NOT available online**  
OTHER TRACES OF THE GRIFFEL FAMILY'S UNDERTAKINGS, held in Polish state archives and not available online. IN THE ARCHIVE OF MODERN RECORDS (AAN): 1) Nationalisation of the undertaking Bracia Griffel — Skawina Oil Refinery, 1946; 2) Rock Oil Refinery at Skawina, firm Bracia Griffel, minutes with annexes, 1951; 3) Nationalisation of Rock Oil Refinery Ltd Bracia Griffel at Skawina, handover protocol, 1950. IN THE NATIONAL ARCHIVE IN KRAKÓW: 1) Papers of the composition case of EISIG CHAIM GRIFFEL AND JAKUB GRIFFEL; 2) Case: Oskar Bruder against DAWID MENDEL GRIFFEL; 3) Griffel Eisig Chaim, deceased — the State Treasury against his estate for court costs, 1935-1936; 4) Mineral oil refineries: Griffel and Co. Skawina, 1936-1937; 5) Griffel Zygmunt — the Muszyna Sawmill Company Enoch and Drenger at Muszyna, on the exclusion of movables from treasury execution, 1933-1934; 6) The firm Griffel and Co. at Skawina against the State Treasury / Polish State Railways for payment, 1927-1928; 7) "Zarszyn" steam sawmills and box factory — Zygmunt Griffel, commercial correspondence, 1932-1934; 8) Bracia Griffel — Rock Oil Refinery Ltd at Skawina, nationalisation, 1949-1950; 9) The firm "Skawina Refineries — Griffel and Co." against the Polish State Railways for compensation, 1927-1930; 10) Complaint of Aleksander Lachs concerning the bailiff's actions as to the creditor M. Frauenglas and the debtors the Skawina Refinery and the estate of E. Ch. Griffel; 11) Frauenglas Michał against the estate of Eisig Chaim Griffel, 1935; 12) Advocate Marian Frauenglas against the estate of Eisig Chaim Griffel, 1933-1935. IN THE ARCHIVE AT WROCŁAW, KAMIENIEC ZĄBKOWICKI BRANCH, in the collection "W. Schimmelpfeng information bureau — German credit-intelligence company": files concerning GRIFFEL LEIZOR, Bolechów; GRIFFEL SCHAJA, Bolechów, 1925-1931; GRIFFEL DAVID MENDEL, Bolechów, 1925-1928. IN THE STATE ARCHIVE AT ZIELONA GÓRA: the civil case Messerschmidt against Griffel. IN THE STATE ARCHIVE AT RZESZÓW, SANOK BRANCH: 1) Handover protocol for the taking over by the state of the Rock Oil Refinery "Bracia Griffel" Ltd at Skawina, 1945-1951; 2) "Zarszyn" Steam Sawmills and Box Factory Ltd, application for entry in the commercial register, 1930-1943. IN THE STATE ARCHIVE AT RADOM: "GRIFFEL HENRYK OSIAS, BORN 16.09.1910 IN STANISŁAWÓW, BACHELOR, LAWYER, ul. CZARNA 9", dated 1941. [That last entry is the one to chase. It places Ozjasz Henryk Griffel — the youngest of the four brothers, entry 4 on the wartime rescue list — in Radom in 1941, described as a lawyer and unmarried, two years before he reaches Jersey City. Nothing else in this archive knows where he was in 1941.]
*the research map · not yet read · OZJASZ IN RADOM 1941*

# Brief for the film — "Dov at Eighty"

**For whoever makes the film, human or LLM. Read this first, then read the manifest.**

- The archive: **https://rapaportfamily.github.io**
- The manifest — everything, in one file: **https://rapaportfamily.github.io/data/film_manifest.json**
- This brief: **https://rapaportfamily.github.io/docs/FILM_BRIEF.md**

Generated 11 August 2026. Runtime 7–10 minutes. Occasion: Dov Bernard Rapaport turns 80 on
28 August 2026.

---

## Your source is this website and nothing else

Start with the manifest. It holds every chapter with its sources, all **126 documents** with
their decoded fields and file paths, **188 people**, both family trees (**1,442 Rapaports**,
**390 Griffels**), **15 memoir plates**, **120 journey photographs**, **60 unidentified**,
**45 timeline events**, **49 places**, **18 open questions**, and a conflicts list. Read all of
it before writing a word. Anything you cannot trace to it does not go in the film.

## The man this is for

Chronologically eighty; in spirit twenty-eight — energetic, sharp, physically strong, still
arguing. He says one thing repeatedly: he wishes he could take his mother Lusia back to those
places, wheel her there on a gurney if that is what it took, and have her show him where she
lived, what happened, how they survived.

**That wish is the spine of the film.** He cannot take her. So the film does it for him: it goes
to the places, opens the documents, and lays her book beside the evidence.

## Hard rules — not style notes

1. **Never invent.** Every claim traces to the manifest. No invented dialogue, no invented
   dates, no "they must have felt".
2. **Show conflicts as conflicts.** This archive publishes disagreements instead of tidying
   them. Berish's death: two camp records say **29 March 1942**; the Yad Vashem page **David
   himself filed** says 1940. Show both. Same for Rebeka (1888/1892, 1942/1943) and Lotte
   (1915/1914).
3. **The memoir is testimony, not proof — treat it with respect.** Where documents confirm
   Lusia, say so plainly. Where nothing corroborates her, say "no document we hold corroborates
   this" — and immediately say why: the people who would have kept the records are the people
   who were killed. Never imply she was wrong for lacking paper.
4. **Living people: no birth dates.**
5. **Never caption an "unidentified" photograph with a name.** Sixty faces nobody can identify —
   show them as exactly that.
6. **Illustration is welcome but must be visibly marked.** Real photograph and AI illustration
   must never be mistaken for each other.
7. **A document is not a face.** Every portrait in the data was looked at by eye on 19 August
   2026, and forty of the seventy-four "portraits" on the two big trees turned out to be
   passports, qualification certificates, Yad Vashem pages, a gravestone, a Bremen
   missing-persons card. They were demoted. A picture is a person's face only where the record
   carries `photo_verified`. Everything else is a paper about them, and must be captioned as a
   paper about them. The film pack PDF prints verified faces only, so if you work from that file
   you cannot get this wrong.

## Structure — macro to micro

| # | Time | Beat |
|---|------|------|
| 1 | 0:00–0:40 | **Cold open.** Dov today, the gurney line in his own words. Cut to the oldest thing we own: Abraham Abe Rapaport, born 1784, and his gravestone. |
| 2 | 0:40–1:30 | **The wide tree.** 1,442 people, 480 families, six generations of Galicia, collapsing inward to four. Register scans as texture: 27 birth, 11 marriage, 19 death records, 1818–1934. |
| 3 | 1:30–2:40 | **The generation before.** Berish's face — the 1924 portrait and the certificate it was pasted onto, listing "Mendel 12 lat, Lotte 8 lat". Rebeka Griffel, and her parents Lajzor Griffel and Sara Matel Chajes, from Jacob's tree. |
| 4 | 2:40–4:00 | **David, Lusia, Shimon.** Nadwórna 1911, Bolechów 1913, the 1938 marriage. David has a face — the wedding and picnic plates. |
| 5 | 4:00–5:30 | **The war.** Lusia's account: the ghetto, the wagon, the false papers, Shimon in a crate for two and a half years. Then the paper: Berish, prisoner 27310, transport 689 — fifty-five men from the prison at Tarnów — arrived 27 March 1942, dead on 29 March. Two days. And Lotte, betrayed in the Lwów ghetto over a forged US-residency paper. |
| 6 | 5:30–6:10 | **The correction.** In 1953, from Haifa, David filed three Pages of Testimony — 53072 his father, 53074 his mother, 53075 his sister — signing them *his son, her son, her brother*. He got his father's year wrong by two. This archive had claimed for months that nobody ever filed them, while holding both pages in its own filing cabinet. |
| 7 | 6:10–7:00 | **Getting out.** Katowice and the separation nobody recorded; Brussels; Dov born 28 August 1946; the DP card — forestry engineer, four languages, destination "Palestine or U.S.A." Then Sète, the *Theodor Herzl*, Cyprus, Haifa. |
| 8 | 7:00–8:00 | **Going back.** Chapter 10. Dov in Warsaw, Przemyśl, Brussels, Katowice with Doron, 2015–2019, 120 photographs. |
| 9 | 8:00–8:40 | **The researchers.** Light, warm, fast. |
| 10 | 8:40–9:30 | **Close.** Back to Dov at 80. He couldn't take her, so they went and found her. |

## The line to use in beat 8

> Every other chapter here was assembled from what other people wrote down — clerks,
> registrars, a camp administration. This is the only one where somebody in the story goes back
> and looks for himself. Nothing in these pictures was recovered. They were made.

## The research story — where the film breathes

**Basia** in Poland rebuilt the paternal line back to 1784 and mentioned in passing that the
printed tree is **five metres long**. **Jacob** sent the maternal side — 390 Griffels, and not
one of his 569 files overlapped hers. **Ms. Kasia** at the Jewish Historical Institute in
Warsaw found David's birth certificate and corrected a birthplace a Brussels clerk had misheard
in 1946. And the archive kept catching itself: it filed a 1924 certificate as "a different
family" when the form plainly names Mendel aged 12 and Lotte aged 8; it announced nobody had
remembered Berish and Rebeka while holding the proof; it found two Charlottes born in Vienna a
year apart and nearly merged them.

## Where the humour lives

It must come from the material, never from the tragedy.

- Jacob's folder of forty-seven strangers, named simply **"Photobook2"**.
- Dov photographing Przemyśl **through a car windscreen, in the rain — forty near-identical
  frames**. He was not going to miss it.
- David's destination box: **"Palestine or U.S.A."** A man keeping his options open.
- A five-metre family tree that fits in no room in Israel.
- Dov himself: eighty, and the least eighty-year-old person in the film.

**Never a joke within thirty seconds of Auschwitz, Lwów or the crate.**

## Visual instruction

Real assets first, always: memoir plates, portraits, register scans (handwriting in three
alphabets), Yad Vashem forms — show the handwriting, then translate on screen — and Dov's own
photographs. **Every document that appears must be named and explained in one sentence: what it
is, who made it, what it proves.** Use illustration only for what was never photographed — the
crate, the wagon, the prison at Tarnów, the boarding of the *Theodor Herzl* — and mark it.

## Deliverables

Narration script with a source id per line · shot list keyed to real file paths from the
manifest · a list of every illustration and what it stands in for · Hebrew and English versions
(the site carries both, plus Polish and French).

## Do not miss

The wider tree · the register spine · Berish's face, date, and the prison he was taken from ·
Lotte, and that David filed for her · the Griffel line and Rebeka's parents · the memoir
compared with the record · Dov's four journeys · the two things the archive admits it does not
have · the sixty faces nobody can name.

## One warning

The manifest's `open_questions` are **questions, not findings** — eighteen of them: how David
survived 1939–46, where "Mosina" is, Lotte's husband's first name. A film that answers them
invents history. A film that **ends** on them is telling the truth about what this family still
does not know.

---

*By Dalia, Dana, Daniel and Doron Rapaport — with Basia, Jacob, Magda, Kasia and Ms. Kasia of
the Jewish Historical Institute, Warsaw.*
